[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 14.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"95a841","wall_time_seconds":1.0334843,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1649,"output":"---\\r\\nname: receiving-code-review\\r\\ndescription: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Code Review Reception\\r\\n\\r\\n## Overview\\r\\n\\r\\nCode review requires technical evaluation, not emotional performance.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Core principle:** Verify before implementing. Ask before assuming. Technical correctness over social comfort.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The Response Pattern\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nWHEN receiving code review feedback:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. READ: Complete feedback without reacting\\r\\n2. UNDERSTAND: Restate requirement in own words (or ask)\\r\\n3. VERIFY: Check against codebase reality\\r\\n4. EVALUATE: Technically sound for THIS codebase?\\r\\n5. RESPOND: Technical acknowledgment or reasoned pushback\\r\\n6. IMPLEMENT: One item at a time, test each\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Forbidden Responses\\r\\n\\r\\n**NEVER:**\\r\\n- \\"You\'re absolutely right!\\" (explicit instruction-file violation)\\r\\n- \\"Great point!\\" / \\"Excellent feedback!\\" (performative)\\r\\n- \\"Let me implement that now\\" (before verification)\\r\\n\\r\\n**INSTEAD:**\\r\\n- Restate the technical requirement\\r\\n- Ask clarifying questions\\r\\n- Push back with technical reasoning if wrong\\r\\n- Just start working (actions > words)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Handling Unclear Feedback\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nIF any item is unclear:\\r\\n  STOP - do not implement anything yet\\r\\n  ASK for clarification on unclear items\\r\\n\\r\\nWHY: Items may be related. Partial understanding = wrong implementation.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Example:**\\r\\n```\\r\\nyour human partner: \\"Fix 1-6\\"\\r\\nYou understand 1,2,3,6. Unclear on 4,5.\\r\\n\\r\\n❌ WRONG: Implement 1,2,3,6 now, ask about 4,5 later\\r\\n✅ RIGHT: \\"I understand items 1,2,3,6. Need clarification on 4 and 5 before proceeding.\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Source-Specific Handling\\r\\n\\r\\n### From your human partner\\r\\n- **Trusted** - implement after understanding\\r\\n- **Still ask** if scope unclear\\r\\n- **No performative agreement**\\r\\n- **Skip to action** or technical acknowledgment\\r\\n\\r\\n### From External Reviewers\\r\\n```\\r\\nBEFORE implementing:\\r\\n  1. Check: Technically correct for THIS codebase?\\r\\n  2. Check: Breaks existing functionality?\\r\\n  3. Check: Reason for current implementation?\\r\\n  4. Check: Works on all platforms/versions?\\r\\n  5. Check: Does reviewer understand full context?\\r\\n\\r\\nIF suggestion seems wrong:\\r\\n  Push back with technical reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nIF can\'t easily verify:\\r\\n  Say so: \\"I can\'t verify this without [X]. Should I [investigate/ask/proceed]?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\nIF conflicts with your human partner\'s prior decisions:\\r\\n  Stop and discuss with your human partner first\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**your human partner\'s rule:** \\"External feedback - be skeptical, but check carefully\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n## YAGNI Check for \\"Professional\\" Features\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nIF reviewer suggests \\"implementing properly\\":\\r\\n  grep codebase for actual usage\\r\\n\\r\\n  IF unused: \\"This endpoint isn\'t called. Remove it (YAGNI)?\\"\\r\\n  IF used: Then implement properly\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**your human partner\'s rule:** \\"You and reviewer both report to me. If we don\'t need this feature, don\'t add it.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n## Implementation Order\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nFOR multi-item feedback:\\r\\n  1. Clarify anything unclear FIRST\\r\\n  2. Then implement in this order:\\r\\n     - Blocking issues (breaks, security)\\r\\n     - Simple fixes (typos, imports)\\r\\n     - Complex fixes (refactoring, logic)\\r\\n  3. Test each fix individually\\r\\n  4. Verify no regressions\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## When To Push Back\\r\\n\\r\\nPush back when:\\r\\n- Suggestion breaks existing functionality\\r\\n- Reviewer lacks full context\\r\\n- Violates YAGNI (unused feature)\\r\\n- Technically incorrect for this stack\\r\\n- Legacy/compatibility reasons exist\\r\\n- Conflicts with your human partner\'s architectural decisions\\r\\n\\r\\n**How to push back:**\\r\\n- Use technical reasoning, not defensiveness\\r\\n- Ask specific questions\\r\\n- Reference working tests/code\\r\\n- Involve your human partner if architectural\\r\\n\\r\\n**If you\'re uncomfortable pushing back out loud:** Name that tension, then tell your partner about the issue you\'ve seen. They\'ll appreciate your honesty.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Acknowledging Correct Feedback\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen feedback IS correct:\\r\\n```\\r\\n✅ \\"Fixed. [Brief description of what changed]\\"\\r\\n✅ \\"Good catch - [specific issue]. Fixed in [location].\\"\\r\\n✅ [Just fix it and show in the code]\\r\\n\\r\\n❌ \\"You\'re absolutely right!\\"\\r\\n❌ \\"Great point!\\"\\r\\n❌ \\"Thanks for catching that!\\"\\r\\n❌ \\"Thanks for [anything]\\"\\r\\n❌ ANY gratitude expression\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Why no thanks:** Actions speak. Just fix it. The code itself shows you heard the feedback.\\r\\n\\r\\n**If you catch yourself about to write \\"Thanks\\":** DELETE IT. State the fix instead.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Gracefully Correcting Your Pushback\\r\\n\\r\\nIf you pushed back and were wrong:\\r\\n```\\r\\n✅ \\"You were right - I checked [X] and it does [Y]. Implementing now.\\"\\r\\n✅ \\"Verified this and you\'re correct. My initial understanding was wrong because [reason]. Fixing.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n❌ Long apology\\r\\n❌ Defending why you pushed back\\r\\n❌ Over-explaining\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nState the correction factually and move on.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common Mistakes\\r\\n\\r\\n| Mistake | Fix |\\r\\n|---------|-----|\\r\\n| Performative agreement | State requirement or just act |\\r\\n| Blind implementation | Verify against codebase first |\\r\\n| Batch without testing | One at a time, test each |\\r\\n| Assuming reviewer is right | Check if breaks things |\\r\\n| Avoiding pushback | Technical correctness > comfort |\\r\\n| Partial implementation | Clarify all items first |\\r\\n| Can\'t verify, proceed anyway | State limitation, ask for direction |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Real Examples\\r\\n\\r\\n**Performative Agreement (Bad):**\\r\\n```\\r\\nReviewer: \\"Remove legacy code\\"\\r\\n❌ \\"You\'re absolutely right! Let me remove that...\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Technical Verification (Good):**\\r\\n```\\r\\nReviewer: \\"Remove legacy code\\"\\r\\n✅ \\"Checking... build target is 10.15+, this API needs 13+. Need legacy for backward compat. Current impl has wrong bundle ID - fix it or drop pre-13 support?\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**YAGNI (Good):**\\r\\n```\\r\\nReviewer: \\"Implement proper metrics tracking with database, date filters, CSV export\\"\\r\\n✅ \\"Grepped codebase - nothing calls this endpoint. Remove it (YAGNI)? Or is there usage I\'m missing?\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Unclear Item (Good):**\\r\\n```\\r\\nyour human partner: \\"Fix items 1-6\\"\\r\\nYou understand 1,2,3,6. Unclear on 4,5.\\r\\n✅ \\"Understand 1,2,3,6. Need clarification on 4 and 5 before implementing.\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## GitHub Thread Replies\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen replying to inline review comments on GitHub, reply in the comment thread (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/{pr}/comments/{id}/replies`), not as a top-level PR comment.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The Bottom Line\\r\\n\\r\\n**External feedback = suggestions to evaluate, not orders to follow.**\\r\\n\\r\\nVerify. Question. Then implement.\\r\\n\\r\\nNo performative agreement. Technical rigor always.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"04abb6","wall_time_seconds":0.6987345,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":5538,"output":"---\\r\\nname: send-booking-link\\r\\ndescription: >\\r\\n  Draft the booking-link email to a student and log the follow-up loop. Use\\r\\n  when Joshua says \\"send <student> their booking link\\", \\"send booking links to\\r\\n  X, Y, Z\\", \\"get <student> scheduled\\", or asks who still needs one. Wraps the\\r\\n  lookup / draft / send / log pipeline that has been hand-orchestrated one\\r\\n  student at a time. Drafts by default; sends only on an explicit go-ahead.\\r\\nallowed-tools: Bash Read Write Edit Glob Grep\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Send booking link\\r\\n\\r\\nFour steps that were being done by hand, seven times in a single session:\\r\\nlook up the email, draft in Joshua\'s voice, send, log the open loop. This does\\r\\nall four, in order, for one student or a batch.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Look up — never guess an address\\r\\n\\r\\nThe roster is generated; rebuild it first so it reflects current folders.\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\npython3 system/scripts/build-roster.py\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThen read `students/roster.generated.md` for the student\'s **email** and\\r\\n**booking link**. Both must be present before drafting.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No email recorded** → stop. Do not guess, do not search for a plausible\\r\\n  address. Tell Joshua the student\'s page has no `email:` in frontmatter.\\r\\n- **No booking pass** → stop. The roster\'s \\"No booking pass\\" section lists\\r\\n  these. Booking access has to be granted before there is anything to send.\\r\\n- **Pass `exhausted`** → stop, and say so. The link is live but has no credits\\r\\n  left, so the student lands on \\"you\'ve used all your sessions\\". Sending it is\\r\\n  worse than sending nothing. Credits have to be added first.\\r\\n- **The URL slug is not the folder slug.** `yahunatan-zetvy` is\\r\\n  `/book/yahunathan`. Always read it from the roster; never derive it.\\r\\n- **Email disagrees with the backend** → stop and ask which is current. The\\r\\n  roster surfaces these rather than picking one.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe roster\'s booking columns are read **live** from `booking_passes` on every\\r\\nrebuild, so they are current as of the rebuild and revoked passes are already\\r\\nexcluded. That is why step 1 rebuilds first.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Draft in Joshua\'s voice\\r\\n\\r\\nRoute through `wiki/teaching-voice.md`. This is student-facing copy, so the\\r\\nvoice rules are not optional.\\r\\n\\r\\nShape: short, warm, no overselling. Own any delay in the first line if there\\r\\nhas been one, give the real reason in a clause, move to the next step. Hand\\r\\nthem the link plainly. No em-dashes. No AI-slop vocabulary.\\r\\n\\r\\nPersonalize from the student\'s folder — `teaching-notes.md`, `open-loops.md`,\\r\\ntheir `<slug>.md` — so the email refers to where they actually are (what they\\r\\nwere last working on, what they said they wanted). A generic blast is the thing\\r\\nthis skill exists to avoid.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Draft, then stop\\r\\n\\r\\n**Default is draft, not send.** Anything a student or prospect reads is drafted\\r\\nand never sent unless it is a pre-approved template and Joshua has said go.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe safety flag is `--draft`. It is the difference between a draft and a sent\\r\\nemail, so it is never omitted on the first pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\ngws gmail +send --to <email> --subject \\"<subject>\\" --body \\"<text>\\" --draft\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nShow Joshua the drafted text. Send only after an explicit go-ahead, and only\\r\\nfor the students he named — the same command with `--draft` removed:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\ngws gmail +send --to <email> --subject \\"<subject>\\" --body \\"<text>\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `--dry-run` first if you are unsure the command is well-formed. Note\\r\\n`--body` takes the text inline, not a file path.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor a batch, draft **all** of them and present the set for one approval, rather\\r\\nthan asking per student. Never send a batch on a single \\"yes\\" unless he says\\r\\n\\"send them all\\" plainly.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Log the loop\\r\\n\\r\\nAppend to `students/<slug>/open-loops.md`, matching the existing format:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n- [YYYY-MM-DD] Sent booking-link email (/book/<slug>). Loop: confirm they schedule; follow up if no booking within ~5 days.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the real date (`date +%F`). If it was drafted but not sent, say `Drafted`\\r\\nrather than `Sent` — a wrong entry there is worse than no entry.\\r\\n\\r\\n`open-loops.md` is a **primary** file: append, never rewrite.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen append one line to `wiki/send-ledger.md`, which is the single surface that\\r\\nanswers \\"did X get their email\\":\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n| YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM | <slug> | booking link (/book/<slug>) | drafted | — |\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nStatus becomes `sent` only when it actually goes out. The per-student log\\r\\ncarries the follow-up loop; the ledger carries whether it was sent.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hard rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Never send without an explicit go-ahead.** Draft is the default, always.\\r\\n- **Never invent an email address or a booking slug.** Missing data stops the\\r\\n  run; it does not get filled in with a guess.\\r\\n- **Never write to `*.generated.md`.** The roster is rebuilt, not edited.\\r\\n- **Log what actually happened.** Drafted is not Sent. If a send fails, log the\\r\\n  failure rather than the intent.\\r\\n- One student\'s email never mentions another student.\\r\\n- If Joshua asks \\"who still needs a booking link\\", answer from the roster\'s\\r\\n  gap sections — that is what they are for — and do not draft anything until\\r\\n  he picks.\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: setup-matt-pocock-skills\\r\\ndescription: \\"Configure this repo for the engineering skills: set up its issue tracker, triage label vocabulary, and domain doc layout. Run once before first use of the other engineering skills.\\"\\r\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Setup Matt Pocock\'s Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nScaffold the per-repo configuration that the engineering skills assume:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Issue tracker**: where issues live (GitHub by default; local markdown is also supported out of the box)\\r\\n- **Triage labels**: the strings used for the five canonical triage roles\\r\\n- **Domain docs**: where `GLOSSARY.md` and ADRs live, and the consumer rules for reading them\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a prompt-driven skill, not a deterministic script. Explore, present what you found, confirm with the user, then write.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Process\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Explore\\r\\n\\r\\nLook at the current repo to understand its starting state. Read whatever exists; don\'t assume:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `git remote -v` and `.git/config`: is this a GitHub repo? Which one?\\r\\n- `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` at the repo root: does either exist? Is there already an `## Agent skills` section in either?\\r\\n- `GLOSSARY.md` and `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` at the repo root\\r\\n- `docs/adr/` and any `src/*/docs/adr/` directories\\r\\n- `docs/agents/`: does this skill\'s prior output already exist?\\r\\n- `.scratch/`: a sign that a local-markdown issue tracker convention is already in use\\r\\n- Is the `triage` skill installed? (a `triage` skill folder alongside this one, or `triage` in your available skills.) This decides whether Section B runs at all.\\r\\n- Monorepo signals: a `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, a `workspaces` field in `package.json`, or a populated `packages/*` with its own `src/`. These are present only in a genuinely large multi-package repo; their absence means single-context, which is almost every repo.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Present findings and ask\\r\\n\\r\\nSummarise what\'s present and what\'s missing. Then take the sections in order. One section, one answer, then the next.\\r\\n\\r\\nLead each section with the recommended answer so the user can accept it in a word. Give a one-line explainer only when the choice genuinely branches; skip the section entirely when exploration already settled it (Section B when `triage` isn\'t installed, Section C when there\'s no monorepo).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Section A: Issue tracker.**\\r\\n\\r\\n> Explainer: The \\"issue tracker\\" is where issues live for this repo. Skills like `to-tickets`, `triage`, and `to-spec` read from and write to it. They need to know whether to call `gh issue create`, write a markdown file under `.scratch/`, or follow some other workflow you describe. Pick the place you actually track work for this repo.\\r\\n\\r\\nDefault posture: these skills were designed for GitHub. If a `git remote` points at GitHub, propose that. If a `git remote` points at GitLab (`gitlab.com` or a self-hosted host), propose GitLab. Otherwise (or if the user prefers), offer:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **GitHub**: issues live in the repo\'s GitHub Issues (uses the `gh` CLI)\\r\\n- **GitLab**: issues live in the repo\'s GitLab Issues (uses the [`glab`](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/cli) CLI)\\r\\n- **Local markdown**: issues live as files under `.scratch/<feature>/` in this repo (good for solo projects or repos without a remote)\\r\\n- **Other** (Jira, Linear, etc.): ask the user to describe the workflow in one paragraph; the skill will record it as freeform prose\\r\\n\\r\\nRecord the choice in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`. The GitHub and GitLab templates carry a \\"PRs as a request surface\\" flag, defaulted **off**. Leave it off and don\'t raise it: a user who wants external PRs in the triage queue can flip the flag in the file later.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Section B: Triage label vocabulary.** Skip this section entirely if the `triage` skill isn\'t installed (exploration told you), since an uninstalled skill needs no labels.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf it is installed, ask exactly one question:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Do you want to keep the default triage labels? (recommended: **yes**)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe defaults are the five canonical roles, each label string equal to its name: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. On **yes**, write them as-is. Only if the user says no, usually because their tracker already uses other names (e.g. `bug:triage` for `needs-triage`), collect the overrides so `triage` applies existing labels instead of creating duplicates.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Section C: Domain docs.** Default to **single-context** (one `GLOSSARY.md` + `docs/adr/` at the repo root). This fits almost every repo; write it without asking.\\r\\n\\r\\nOffer **multi-context** (a root `GLOSSARY-MAP.md` pointing to per-context `GLOSSARY.md` files) only when exploration found monorepo signals. Then confirm which layout they want.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Confirm and edit\\r\\n\\r\\nShow the user a draft of:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The `## Agent skills` block to add to whichever of `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` is being edited (see step 4 for selection rules)\\r\\n- The contents of `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, `docs/agents/domain.md`, and `docs/agents/triage-labels.md` (the last only when `triage` is installed)\\r\\n\\r\\nLet them edit before writing.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Write\\r\\n\\r\\n**Pick the file to edit:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- If `CLAUDE.md` exists, edit it.\\r\\n- Else if `AGENTS.md` exists, edit it.\\r\\n- If neither exists, ask the user which one to create; don\'t pick for them.\\r\\n\\r\\nNever create `AGENTS.md` when `CLAUDE.md` already exists (or vice versa); always edit the one that\'s already there.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf an `## Agent skills` block already exists in the chosen file, update its contents in-place rather than appending a duplicate. Don\'t overwrite user edits to the surrounding sections.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe block:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\n[one-line summary of where issues are tracked]. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\n[one-line summary of the label vocabulary]. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\n[one-line summary of layout: \\"single-context\\" or \\"multi-context\\"]. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nInclude the `### Triage labels` sub-block, and write `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`, only when `triage` is installed and Section B ran. When it isn\'t, both are omitted.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen Section B ran on GitHub or GitLab, create each configured label the tracker lacks (`gh label create` / `glab label create`).\\r\\n\\r\\nThen write the docs files using the seed templates in this skill folder as a starting point:\\r\\n\\r\\n- [issue-tracker-github.md](./issue-tracker-github.md): GitHub issue tracker\\r\\n- [issue-tracker-gitlab.md](./issue-tracker-gitlab.md): GitLab issue tracker\\r\\n- [issue-tracker-local.md](./issue-tracker-local.md): local-markdown issue tracker\\r\\n- [triage-labels.md](./triage-labels.md): label mapping (only if `triage` is installed)\\r\\n- [domain.md](./domain.md): domain doc consumer rules + layout\\r\\n\\r\\nFor \\"other\\" issue trackers, write `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` from scratch using the user\'s description.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Done\\r\\n\\r\\nTell the user the setup is complete and which engineering skills will now read from these files. Mention they can edit `docs/agents/*.md` directly later; re-running this skill is only necessary if they want to switch issue trackers or restart from scratch.\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: setup-pre-commit\\r\\ndescription: Set up Husky pre-commit hooks with lint-staged (Prettier), type checking, and tests in the current repo. Use when user wants to add pre-commit hooks, set up Husky, configure lint-staged, or add commit-time formatting/typechecking/testing.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Setup Pre-Commit Hooks\\r\\n\\r\\n## What This Sets Up\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Husky** pre-commit hook\\r\\n- **lint-staged** running Prettier on all staged files\\r\\n- **Prettier** config (if missing)\\r\\n- **typecheck** and **test** scripts in the pre-commit hook\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Detect package manager\\r\\n\\r\\nCheck for `package-lock.json` (npm), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (pnpm), `yarn.lock` (yarn), `bun.lockb` (bun). Use whichever is present. Default to npm if unclear.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Install dependencies\\r\\n\\r\\nInstall as devDependencies:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nhusky lint-staged prettier\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Initialize Husky\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpx husky init\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis creates `.husky/` dir and adds `prepare: \\"husky\\"` to package.json.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Create `.husky/pre-commit`\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite this file (no shebang needed for Husky v9+):\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nnpx lint-staged\\r\\nnpm run typecheck\\r\\nnpm run test\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Adapt**: Replace `npm` with detected package manager. If repo has no `typecheck` or `test` script in package.json, omit those lines and tell the user.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Create `.lintstagedrc`\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"*\\": \\"prettier --ignore-unknown --write\\"\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 6. Create `.prettierrc` (if missing)\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly create if no Prettier config exists. Use these defaults:\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"useTabs\\": false,\\r\\n  \\"tabWidth\\": 2,\\r\\n  \\"printWidth\\": 80,\\r\\n  \\"singleQuote\\": false,\\r\\n  \\"trailingComma\\": \\"es5\\",\\r\\n  \\"semi\\": true,\\r\\n  \\"arrowParens\\": \\"always\\"\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 7. Verify\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] `.husky/pre-commit` exists and is executable\\r\\n- [ ] `.lintstagedrc` exists\\r\\n- [ ] `prepare` script in package.json is `\\"husky\\"`\\r\\n- [ ] `prettier` config exists\\r\\n- [ ] Run `npx lint-staged` to verify it works\\r\\n\\r\\n### 8. Commit\\r\\n\\r\\nStage all changed/created files and commit with message: `Add pre-commit hooks (husky + lint-staged + prettier)`\\r\\n\\r\\nThis will run through the new pre-commit hooks: a good smoke test that everything works.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n\\r\\n- Husky v9+ doesn\'t need shebangs in hook files\\r\\n- `prettier --ignore-unknown` skips files Prettier can\'t parse (images, etc.)\\r\\n- The pre-commit runs lint-staged first (fast, staged-only), then full typecheck and tests\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: setup-ts-deep-modules\\r\\ndescription: Wire dependency-cruiser into a TypeScript repo so each package is a deep module, with implementation hidden in subfolders and reachable only through its entry-point files. User-invoked.\\r\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Setup TS Deep Modules\\r\\n\\r\\nMake every package in this repo a **deep module**: a lot of behaviour behind a small interface. A package\'s public surface is its **entry points** (the files at the package root), and everything in its subfolders is hidden. This skill installs [dependency-cruiser](https://github.com/sverweij/dependency-cruiser) and the rules that make the entry points the only way in, then proves the rules bite.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor the vocabulary (deep module, interface, seam, depth), call the Skill tool with \\"codebase-design\\" and use its language throughout.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The shape this enforces\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\nsrc/packages/\\r\\n  <name>/\\r\\n    index.ts        ← an entry point (public). Import this from outside.\\r\\n    client.ts       ← another entry point. Packages may expose SEVERAL.\\r\\n    lib/            ← implementation: hidden from outside, free to import each other.\\r\\n    tests/          ← co-located tests + fixtures (a subfolder, so private).\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe public surface is the package\'s **root files**, not one designated `index.ts`. By convention implementation lives in `lib/` and tests in `tests/`, giving every package the same two-folder shape. The rule itself is general, though: *anything* in *any* subfolder is private, so you never extend the config to add a folder.\\r\\n\\r\\nFour rules, all `error`:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Entry-point boundary**: code outside a package (app code or another package) may import only that package\'s entry points (its root files), never anything in its subfolders.\\r\\n2. **Intra-package freedom**: a package\'s own files import each other freely.\\r\\n3. **Tests through the entry points**: files under `<pkg>/tests/` may import any package\'s entry points and their own `tests/` fixtures, but never any package\'s subfolder internals (not even their own). Integration tests across packages are fine; deep imports are not.\\r\\n4. **No cycles**: no dependency cycles.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Entry points, not a barrel.** Because the public surface is *every* root file, a package can expose several small entry points (`index.ts`, `client.ts`, `server.ts`) instead of funnelling everything through one giant `index.ts`. Barrel files that re-export a whole subtree are discouraged; keep entry points small and hide implementation in subfolders.\\r\\n\\r\\nLayering (which packages may depend on which) is a *different* concern and is left as a commented stub in the config for this repo to fill in.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Steps\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Detect the environment\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Package manager**: `pnpm-lock.yaml` → pnpm, `yarn.lock` → yarn, `bun.lockb` → bun, else npm. Use it for every command below (`pnpm`/`yarn`/`npm run`/`bunx`).\\r\\n- **Packages root**: if `src/` exists use `src/packages`, else `packages`. Confirm the choice with the user if the repo already has a different obvious convention.\\r\\n- **Existing config**: check for a `.dependency-cruiser.*` file. If one exists, do **not** overwrite it: merge the four rules and the options in, and tell the user what you added.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** package manager, packages root, and existing-config status are all known.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Install dependency-cruiser\\r\\n\\r\\nInstall `dependency-cruiser` as a devDependency with the detected package manager.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** `dependency-cruiser` is in `devDependencies`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Write the config\\r\\n\\r\\nCopy [`dependency-cruiser.config.cjs`](./dependency-cruiser.config.cjs) to the repo root as `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`. Set `PACKAGES_ROOT` to the root detected in step 1. The rules are path-depth based and extension-agnostic, so nothing else needs adapting.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` exists with the correct `PACKAGES_ROOT`, and the four forbidden rules are present.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Wire it into the checks\\r\\n\\r\\n- Add a `lint:boundaries` script: `depcruise <packages-root>` (or `depcruise src`).\\r\\n- Fold it into the repo\'s umbrella check command, the one that already runs typecheck (e.g. a `check` / `ci` / `validate` script). Do **not** touch `tsconfig` or add path aliases.\\r\\n- If there is no umbrella script, add `lint:boundaries` and tell the user to include it in CI.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** `lint:boundaries` exists and runs as part of the same command as typecheck.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Scaffold the example package\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate a committed `<packages-root>/example/` as a copy-me template:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `index.ts` is an entry point. Export one function that delegates to an internal file (so the package is visibly *deep*, not a pass-through).\\r\\n- `lib/impl.ts`: an internal file in a **subfolder**, imported by `index.ts`, not reachable from outside.\\r\\n- `tests/example.test.ts` imports **only** `../index` (an entry point) and asserts against the public function.\\r\\n\\r\\nTell the user this is a starter template to copy or delete.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** the example package exists, exposes its behaviour through a root entry point, and hides `impl` in a subfolder.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 6. Prove the rules bite\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is the completion criterion for the whole skill: a config that doesn\'t fail on a violation is worthless.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Run `lint:boundaries`. It must **pass** on the clean example.\\r\\n2. Temporarily add a deep import to `tests/example.test.ts` (e.g. `import { thing } from \\"../lib/impl\\"`). Run `lint:boundaries` again; it must **fail** with `tests-through-entrypoints`.\\r\\n3. Revert the deep import. Run once more, and it must **pass**.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** you have observed a pass, then a fail on the deep import, then a pass again. If step 2 does not fail, the rules are not wired correctly, so fix before finishing.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 7. Document the convention\\r\\n\\r\\nWrite a `README.md` **in the packages folder** (`<packages-root>/README.md`, next to the packages it governs) covering: the `src/packages/<name>/` layout (entry points at the root, `lib/` for implementation, `tests/` for tests), \\"import only through a package\'s entry points (its root files)\\", and how to run `lint:boundaries`. **Discourage barrel files** explicitly: expose several small entry points instead of re-exporting a whole subtree through one index. Keep it to the copy-me snippet plus the four rules in one paragraph each.\\r\\n\\r\\nThen add a **context pointer** to it from the repo\'s agent-instructions file (`CLAUDE.md` if present, else `AGENTS.md`, creating `AGENTS.md` if neither exists). One line is enough, e.g. `Packages are deep modules: see [src/packages/README.md](./src/packages/README.md) before adding or importing one.` This is what makes an agent discover the boundary rule instead of tripping over it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Done when:** `<packages-root>/README.md` exists and discourages barrels, and the repo\'s `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` links to it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n\\r\\n- The config\'s `$1` back-references (dependency-cruiser\'s group matching) are what let a package reach its own internals while outsiders can\'t. Don\'t flatten them into separate per-package rules.\\r\\n- Public vs private is decided by **depth**: a package\'s root files are entry points; anything in a subfolder is private. The conventional subfolders are `lib/` (implementation) and `tests/`, but the rule doesn\'t hardcode them: any subfolder is private, so a new folder never needs a config change. Adding an entry point is just adding a root file (no barrel).\\r\\n- Packages are **flat**: one tier of immediate children under the root. A package\'s internals may nest as deep as you like; a package may not contain another package.\\r\\n- Use `.cjs` (not `.js`) so the config\'s `module.exports` works even in `\\"type\\": \\"module\\"` repos.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 7.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"3bbbc9","wall_time_seconds":1.0132535,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":8072,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 8072)\\nTotal output lines: 554\\n\\n---\\r\\nname: shadcn\\r\\ndescription: Manages shadcn components and projects — adding, searching, fixing, debugging, styling, and composing UI. Provides project context, component docs, and usage examples. Applies when working with shadcn/ui, component registries, presets, --preset codes, or any project with a components.json file. Also triggers for \\"shadcn init\\", \\"create an app with --preset\\", or \\"switch to --preset\\".\\r\\nuser-invocable: false\\r\\nallowed-tools: Bash(npx shadcn@latest *), Bash(pnpm dlx shadcn@latest *), Bash(bunx --bun shadcn@latest *)\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# shadcn/ui\\r\\n\\r\\nA framework for building ui, components and design systems. Components are added as source code to the user\'s project via the CLI.\\r\\n\\r\\n> **IMPORTANT:** Run all CLI commands using the project\'s package runner: `npx shadcn@latest`, `pnpm dlx shadcn@latest`, or `bunx --bun shadcn@latest` — based on the project\'s `packageManager`. Examples below use `npx shadcn@latest` but substitute the correct runner for the project.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current Project Context\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n!`npx shadcn@latest info --json`\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe JSON above contains the project config and installed components. Use `npx shadcn@latest docs <component>` to get documentation and example URLs for any component.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Principles\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Use existing components first.** Use `npx shadcn@latest search` to check registries before writing custom UI. Check community registries too.\\r\\n2. **Compose, don\'t reinvent.** Settings page = Tabs + Card + form controls. Dashboard = Sidebar + Card + Chart + Table.\\r\\n3. **Use built-in variants before custom styles.** `variant=\\"outline\\"`, `size=\\"sm\\"`, etc.\\r\\n4. **Use semantic colors.** `bg-primary`, `text-muted-foreground` — never raw values like `bg-blue-500`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Critical Rules\\r\\n\\r\\nThese rules are **always enforced**. Each links to a file with Incorrect/Correct code pairs.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Styling & Tailwind → [styling.md](./rules/styling.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`className` for layout, not styling.** Never override component colors or typography.\\r\\n- **No `space-x-*` or `space-y-*`.** Use `flex` with `gap-*`. For vertical stacks, `flex flex-col gap-*`.\\r\\n- **Use `size-*` when width and height are equal.** `size-10` not `w-10 h-10`.\\r\\n- **Use `truncate` shorthand.** Not `overflow-hidden text-ellipsis whitespace-nowrap`.\\r\\n- **No manual `dark:` color overrides.** Use semantic tokens (`bg-background`, `text-muted-foreground`).\\r\\n- **Use `cn()` for conditional classes.** Don\'t write manual template literal ternaries.\\r\\n- **No manual `z-index` on overlay components.** Dialog, Sheet, Popover, etc. handle their own stacking.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Forms & Inputs → [forms.md](./rules/forms.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Forms use `FieldGroup` + `Field`.** Never use raw `div` with `space-y-*` or `grid gap-*` for form layout.\\r\\n- **`InputGroup` uses `InputGroupInput`/`InputGroupTextarea`.** Never raw `Input`/`Textarea` inside `InputGroup`.\\r\\n- **Buttons inside inputs use `InputGroup` + `InputGroupAddon`.**\\r\\n- **Option sets (2–7 choices) use `ToggleGroup`.** Don\'t loop `Button` with manual active state.\\r\\n- **`FieldSet` + `FieldLegend` for grouping related checkboxes/radios.** Don\'t use a `div` with a heading.\\r\\n- **Field validation uses `data-invalid` + `aria-invalid`.** `data-invalid` on `Field`, `aria-invalid` on the control. For disabled: `data-disabled` on `Field`, `disabled` on the control.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Component Structure → [composition.md](./rules/composition.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Items always inside their Group.** `SelectItem` → `SelectGroup`. `DropdownMenuItem` → `DropdownMenuGroup`. `CommandItem` → `CommandGroup`.\\r\\n- **Use `asChild` (radix) or `render` (base) for custom triggers.** Check `base` field from `npx shadcn@latest info`. → [base-vs-radix.md](./rules/base-vs-radix.md)\\r\\n- **Dialog, Sheet, and Drawer always need a Title.** `DialogTitle`, `SheetTitle`, `DrawerTitle` required for accessibility. Use `className=\\"sr-only\\"` if visually hidden.\\r\\n- **Use full Card composition.** `CardHeader`/`CardTitle`/`CardDescription`/`CardContent`/`CardFooter`. Don\'t dump everything in `CardContent`.\\r\\n- **Button has no `isPending`/`isLoading`.** Compose with `Spinner` + `data-icon` + `disabled`.\\r\\n- **`TabsTrigger` must be inside `TabsList`.** Never render triggers directly in `Tabs`.\\r\\n- **`Avatar` always needs `AvatarFallback`.** For when the image fails to load.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Use Components, Not Custom Markup → [composition.md](./rules/composition.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Use existing components before custom markup.** Check if a component exists before writing a styled `div`.\\r\\n- **Callouts use `Alert`.** Don\'t build custom styled divs.\\r\\n- **Empty states use `Empty`.** Don\'t build custom empty state markup.\\r\\n- **Toast via `sonner`.** Use `toast()` from `sonner`.\\r\\n- **Use `Separator`** instead of `<hr>` or `<div className=\\"border-t\\">`.\\r\\n- **Use `Skeleton`** for loading placeholders. No custom `animate-pulse` divs.\\r\\n- **Use `Badge`** instead of custom styled spans.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Icons → [icons.md](./rules/icons.md)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Icons in `Button` use `data-icon`.** `data-icon=\\"inline-start\\"` or `data-icon=\\"inline-end\\"` on the icon.\\r\\n- **No sizing classes on icons inside components.** Components handle icon sizing via CSS. No `size-4` or `w-4 h-4`.\\r\\n- **Pass icons as objects, not string keys.** `icon={CheckIcon}`, not a string lookup.\\r\\n\\r\\n### CLI\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Never decode preset codes or build preset URLs manually.** Use `npx shadcn@latest preset decode <code>`, `preset url <code>`, or `preset open <code>`. For project-aware preset detection, use `npx shadcn@latest preset resolve`.\\r\\n- **Apply preset codes directly with the CLI.** Use `npx shadcn@latest apply <code>` for existing projects, or `npx shadcn@latest init --preset <code>` when initializing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key Patterns\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are the most common patterns that differentiate correct shadcn/ui code. For edge cases, see the linked rule files above.\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n// Form layout: FieldGroup + Field, not div + Label.\\r\\n<FieldGroup>\\r\\n  <Field>\\r\\n    <FieldLabel htmlFor=\\"email\\">Email</FieldLabel>\\r\\n    <Input id=\\"email\\" />\\r\\n  </Field>\\r\\n</FieldGroup>\\r\\n\\r\\n// Validation: data-invalid on Field, aria-invalid on the control.\\r\\n<Field data-invalid>\\r\\n  <FieldLabel>Email</FieldLabel>\\r\\n  <Input aria-invalid />\\r\\n  <FieldDescription>Invalid email.</FieldDescription>\\r\\n</Field>\\r\\n\\r\\n// Icons in buttons: data-icon, no sizing classes.\\r\\n<Button>\\r\\n  <SearchIcon data-icon=\\"inline-start\\" />\\r\\n  Search\\r\\n</Button>\\r\\n\\r\\n// Spacing: gap-*, not space-y-*.\\r\\n<div className=\\"flex flex-col gap-4\\">  // correct\\r\\n<div className=\\"space-y-4\\">           // wrong\\r\\n\\r\\n// Equal dimensions: size-*, not w-* h-*.\\r\\n<Avatar className=\\"size-10\\">   // correct\\r\\n<Avatar className=\\"w-10 h-10\\"> // wrong\\r\\n\\r\\n// Status colors: Badge variants or semantic tokens, not raw colors.\\r\\n<Badge variant=\\"secondary\\">+20.1%</Badge>    // correct\\r\\n<span className=\\"text-emerald-600\\">+20.1%</span> // wrong\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Component Selection\\r\\n\\r\\n| Need                       | Use                                                                                                 |\\r\\n| -------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| Button/action              | `Button` with appropriate variant                                                                   |\\r\\n| Form inputs                | `Input`, `Select`, `Combobox`, `Switch`, `Checkbox`, `RadioGroup`, `Textarea`, `InputOTP`, `Slider` |\\r\\n| Toggle between 2–5 options | `ToggleGroup` + `ToggleGroupItem`                                                                   |\\r\\n| Data display               | `Table`, `Card`, `Badge`, `Avatar`                                                                  |\\r\\n| Navigation                 | `Sidebar`, `NavigationMenu`, `Breadcrumb`, `Tabs`, `Pagination`                                     |\\r\\n| Overlays                   | `Dialog` (modal), `Sheet` (side panel), `Drawer` (bottom sheet), `AlertDialog` (confirmation)       |\\r\\n| Feedback                   | `sonner` (toast), `Alert`, `Progress`, `Skeleton`, `Spinner`                                        |\\r\\n| Command palette            | `Command` inside `Dialog`                                                                           |\\r\\n| Charts                     | `Chart` (wraps Recharts)                                                                            |\\r\\n| Layout                     | `Card`, `Separator`, `Resizable`, `ScrollArea`, `Accordion`, `Collapsible`                          |\\r\\n| Empty states               | `Empty`                                                                                             |\\r\\n| Menus                      | `DropdownMenu`, `ContextMenu`, `Menubar`                                                            |\\r\\n| Tooltips/info              | `Tooltip`, `HoverCard`, `Popover`                                                                   |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key Fields\\r\\n\\r\\nThe injected project context contains these key fields:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`aliases`** → use the actual alias prefix for imports (e.g. `@/`, `~/`), never hardcode.\\r\\n- **`isRSC`** → when `true`, components using `useState`, `useEffect`, event handlers, or browser APIs need `\\"use client\\"` at the top of the file. Always reference this field when advising on the directive.\\r\\n- **`tailwindVersion`** → `\\"v4\\"` uses `@theme inline` blocks; `\\"v3\\"` uses `tailwind.config.js`.\\r\\n- **`tailwindCssFile`** → the global CSS file where custom CSS variables are defined. Always edit this file, never create a new one.\\r\\n- **`style`** → component visual treatment (e.g. `nova`, `vega`).\\r\\n- **`base`** → primitive library (`radix` or `base`). Affects component APIs and available props.\\r\\n- **`iconLibrary`** → determines icon imports. Use `lucide-react` for `lucide`, `@tabler/icons-react` for `tabler`, etc. Never assume `lucide-react`.\\r\\n- **`resolvedPaths`** → exact file-system destinations for components, utils, hooks, etc.\\r\\n- **`framework`** → routing and file conventions (e.g. Next.js App Router vs Vite SPA).\\r\\n- **`packageManager`** → use this for any non-shadcn dependency installs (e.g. `pnpm add date-fns` vs `npm install date-fns`).\\r\\n- **`preset`** → resolved preset code and values for the current project. Use `npx shadcn@latest preset resolve --json` when you only need preset information.\\r\\n\\r\\nSee [cli.md — `info` command](./cli.md) for the full field reference.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Component Docs, Examples, and Usage\\r\\n\\r\\nRun `npx shadcn@latest docs <component>` to get the URLs for a component\'s documentation, examples, and API reference. Fetch these URLs to get the actual content.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpx shadcn@latest docs button dialog select\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**When creating, fixing, debugging, or using a component, always run `npx shadcn@latest docs` and fetch the URLs first.** This ensures you\'re working with the correct API and usage patterns rather than guessing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Get project context** — already injected above. Run `npx shadcn@latest info` again if you need to refresh.\\r\\n2. **Check installed components first** — before running `add`, always check the `components` list from project context or list the `resolvedPaths.ui` directory. Don\'t import components that haven\'t been added, and don\'t re-add ones already installed.\\r\\n3. **Find components** — `npx shadcn@latest search`.\\r\\n4. **Get docs and examples** — run `npx shadcn@latest docs <component>` to get URLs, then fetch them. Use `npx shadcn@latest view` to browse registry items you haven\'t installed. To preview changes to installed components, use `npx shadcn@latest add --diff`.\\r\\n5. **Install or update** — `npx shadcn@latest add`. When updating existing components, use `--dry-run` and `--diff` to preview changes first (see [Updating Components](#updating-components) below).\\r\\n6. **Fix imports in third-party components** — After adding components from community registries (e.g. `@bundui`, `@magicui`), check the added non-UI files for hardcoded import paths like `@/components/ui/...`. These won\'t match the project\'s actual aliases. Use `npx shadcn@latest info` to get the correct `ui` alias (e.g. `@workspace/ui/components`) and rewrite the imports accordingly. The CLI rewrites imports for its own UI files, but third-party registry components may use default paths that don\'t match the project.\\r\\n7. **Review added components** — After adding a component or block from any registry, **always read the added files and verify they are correct**. Check for missing sub-components (e.g. `SelectItem` without `SelectGroup`), missing imports, incorrect composition, or violations of the [Critical Rules](#critical-rules). Also replace any icon imports with the project\'s `iconLibrary` from the project context (e.g. if the registry item uses `lucide-react` but the project uses `hugeicons`, swap the imports and icon names accordingly). Fix all issues before moving on.\\r\\n8. **Registry must be explicit** — When the user asks to add a block or component, **do not guess the registry**. If no registry is specified (e.g. user says \\"add a login block\\" without specifying `@shadcn`, `@tailark`, etc.), ask which registry to use. Never default to a registry on behalf of the user.\\r\\n9. **Switching presets** — Ask the user first: **overwrite**, **partial**, **merge**, or **skip**?\\r\\n   - **Inspect current preset**: `npx shadcn@latest preset resolve`. Use `--json` when you need structured values.\\r\\n   - **Inspect incoming preset**: `npx shadcn@latest preset decode <code>`. Use `preset url <code>` or `preset open <code>` to share or open the preset builder.\\r\\n   - **Overwrite**: `npx shadcn@latest apply <code>`. Overwrites detected components, fonts, and CSS variables.\\r\\n   - **Partial**: `npx shadcn@latest apply <code> --only theme,font`. Updates only the selected preset parts without reinstalling UI components. Supported values are `theme` and `font`; comma-separated combinations are allowed. `icon` is intentionally not supported, because icon changes may require full component reinstall and transforms.\\r\\n   - **Merge**: `npx shadcn@latest init --preset <code> --force --no-reinstall`, then run `npx shadcn@latest info` to list installed components, then for each installed component use `--dry-run` and `--diff` to [smart merge](#updating-components) it individually.\\r\\n   - **Skip**: `npx shadcn@latest init --preset <code> --force --no-reinstall`. Only updates config and CSS, leaves components as-is.\\r\\n   - **Important**: Always run preset commands inside the user\'s project directory. `apply` only works in an existing project with a `components.json` file. The CLI automatically preserves the current base (`base` vs `radix`) from `components.json`. If you must use a scratch/temp directory (e.g. for `--dry-run` comparisons), pass `--base <current-base>` explicitly — preset codes do not encode the base.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Updating Components\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the user asks to update a component from upstream while keeping their local changes, use `--dry-run` and `--diff` to intelligently merge. **NEVER fetch raw files from Gi…572 tokens truncated… `vega`, `maia`, `lyra`, `mira`, `luma`\\r\\n**Templates:** `next`, `vite`, `start`, `react-router`, `astro` (all support `--monorepo`) and `laravel` (not supported for monorepo)\\r\\n**Preset codes:** Version-prefixed base62 strings (e.g. `a2r6bw` or `b0`), from [ui.shadcn.com](https://ui.shadcn.com).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Detailed References\\r\\n\\r\\n- [rules/forms.md](./rules/forms.md) — FieldGroup, Field, InputGroup, ToggleGroup, FieldSet, validation states\\r\\n- [rules/composition.md](./rules/composition.md) — Groups, overlays, Card, Tabs, Avatar, Alert, Empty, Toast, Separator, Skeleton, Badge, Button loading\\r\\n- [rules/icons.md](./rules/icons.md) — data-icon, icon sizing, passing icons as objects\\r\\n- [rules/styling.md](./rules/styling.md) — Semantic colors, variants, className, spacing, size, truncate, dark mode, cn(), z-index\\r\\n- [rules/base-vs-radix.md](./rules/base-vs-radix.md) — asChild vs render, Select, ToggleGroup, Slider, Accordion\\r\\n- [cli.md](./cli.md) — Commands, flags, presets, templates\\r\\n- [customization.md](./customization.md) — Theming, CSS variables, extending components\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: site-teardown\\r\\ndescription: \\"Reverse engineer any website into a complete build blueprint. Analyzes the HTML structure, fetches and extracts all animation/interaction code from the site\'s JavaScript, and pulls the complete design system from the CSS. Outputs a structured teardown document with tech stack, every visual effect and how it\'s implemented, design tokens, assets needed, and a section-by-section build plan. Use this skill whenever the user wants to analyze how a website was built, reverse engineer a site, clone or recreate a website, understand what tech stack or animations a site uses, do a site teardown, deconstruct a page, figure out how an effect works on a website, or says things like \'steal this website\', \'break down this site\', \'how did they build this\', \'website blueprint\', \'analyze this site for me\', or gives you a URL and asks you to figure out how it works. Also trigger when the user pastes raw HTML source code and asks what\'s going on or how to recreate it.\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Site Teardown\\r\\n\\r\\nReverse engineer any website into a complete build blueprint — tech stack, every visual effect with implementation details, full design system, and a section-by-section build plan. The output should be thorough enough that someone could hand it to Claude Code in a fresh session and build a clone without ever visiting the original site.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When This Skill Activates\\r\\n\\r\\n**Activate when the user wants to:**\\r\\n- Analyze how a website was built\\r\\n- Reverse engineer or deconstruct a site\\r\\n- Clone or recreate a website\'s design and animations\\r\\n- Understand what tech stack, libraries, or animation techniques a site uses\\r\\n- Get a build blueprint for a website they want to recreate\\r\\n- Understand how a specific effect on a website works (and the site hasn\'t been analyzed yet)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Example triggers:**\\r\\n- \\"Tear down this site: https://example.com\\"\\r\\n- \\"How did they build this website?\\"\\r\\n- \\"Analyze this site for me\\"\\r\\n- \\"Reverse engineer this page\\"\\r\\n- \\"I want to steal this website\'s design\\"\\r\\n- \\"Clone this site analysis\\"\\r\\n- \\"Website blueprint for [URL]\\"\\r\\n- \\"Break down this page\\"\\r\\n- \\"Deconstruct this site\\"\\r\\n- `/site-teardown`\\r\\n- `/site-teardown [URL]`\\r\\n- User pastes raw HTML and asks how to recreate it\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pipeline\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 1 — Get the HTML\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML is the foundation. It tells you what\'s on the page — the elements, the classes, the structure, the image/video paths, the SVGs.\\r\\n\\r\\n**If the user pastes raw HTML source code:**\\r\\n- Use it directly. Pasted HTML is more complete than a WebFetch for large pages because WebFetch processes content through a sub-model that may summarize details.\\r\\n- Scan the HTML and extract key structural information:\\r\\n  - All section/component class names\\r\\n  - Image and video source paths\\r\\n  - SVG elements (logos, icons, decorative shapes)\\r\\n  - Form structures\\r\\n  - Navigation structure\\r\\n  - Meta tags (author, description, title)\\r\\n  - Any inline styles or inline scripts\\r\\n\\r\\n**If the user only provides a URL:**\\r\\n- Use WebFetch on the URL with this prompt: \\"Return the complete HTML structure of this page. Include all class names, element hierarchy, image/video src paths, SVG elements, meta tags, script src paths, stylesheet href paths, and any inline styles or scripts. Preserve the exact class names and data attributes. Do not summarize the structure — return as much detail as possible.\\"\\r\\n- Note to user: for the most complete analysis, they can paste the raw HTML (Ctrl+U → Ctrl+A → Ctrl+C on the page).\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 2 — Find the JS and CSS File Paths\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the HTML, locate the main application JavaScript and CSS files. These are what contain the actual behavior and styling.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Look for:**\\r\\n```html\\r\\n<!-- CSS - usually in <head> -->\\r\\n<link rel=\\"stylesheet\\" href=\\"/path/to/main.css\\">\\r\\n\\r\\n<!-- JS - usually before </body> -->\\r\\n<script src=\\"/path/to/main.js\\"></script>\\r\\n<script type=\\"module\\" src=\\"/dist/assets/app.js\\"></script>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n**Filter out third-party scripts** — skip these, they\'re not the site\'s custom code:\\r\\n- Google Tag Manager, Google Analytics, gtag\\r\\n- Cookie consent (iubenda, OneTrust, CookieBot)\\r\\n- reCAPTCHA, hCaptcha\\r\\n- CDN-hosted libraries (cdnjs, unpkg, jsdelivr) — note what libraries they use but don\'t fetch them\\r\\n- Social media widgets, chat widgets\\r\\n- WordPress core scripts (`/wp-includes/js/`)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Keep the main application files** — these are what you want:\\r\\n- Files from `/dist/`, `/build/`, `/assets/`, `/static/`\\r\\n- Files with names like `main.js`, `app.js`, `bundle.js`, `index.js`\\r\\n- Same pattern for CSS: `main.css`, `app.css`, `style.css`, `styles.css`\\r\\n- Modulepreload hints also point to important files: `<link rel=\\"modulepreload\\" href=\\"...\\">`\\r\\n\\r\\nConstruct the full URLs by combining the site\'s domain with the file paths.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 3 — Fetch the JavaScript\\r\\n\\r\\nUse WebFetch on the main JS file. The goal is to extract every interaction, animation, and behavior on the site.\\r\\n\\r\\nBuild the prompt dynamically using class names found in the HTML. Start with this base prompt and append the site-specific class names:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Base prompt:**\\r\\n```\\r\\nExtract ALL animation code, event listeners, scroll effects, mouse interactions,\\r\\nclick handlers, and GSAP/animation library usage from this JavaScript file.\\r\\n\\r\\nInclude:\\r\\n- Every function that manipulates the DOM, changes styles, or responds to user input\\r\\n- All ScrollTrigger/scroll-based animation configurations\\r\\n- All timeline definitions and animation sequences\\r\\n- All mousemove/mouseenter/mouseleave/click/touch handlers\\r\\n- All class toggling and DOM manipulation\\r\\n- All IntersectionObserver usage\\r\\n- All CSS custom property manipulation\\r\\n- All Lenis/locomotive/smooth scroll configurations\\r\\n- All Barba.js/Swup/page transition code\\r\\n- All slider/carousel/draggable configurations\\r\\n- All parallax implementations\\r\\n- All image sequence/frame animation logic\\r\\n- All cursor/pointer customization\\r\\n- All preloader/loading animations\\r\\n- All lazy loading logic\\r\\n- All form interaction handlers\\r\\n- All accordion/toggle/menu animations\\r\\n- Custom easing functions and timing values\\r\\n\\r\\nAlso look for any code targeting these class names from the HTML:\\r\\n[INSERT COMMA-SEPARATED LIST OF KEY CLASS NAMES FROM THE HTML]\\r\\n\\r\\nReturn actual code snippets with context about what elements they target.\\r\\nDo not summarize — return as much raw code as possible.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace the class name placeholder with real classes extracted from the HTML in Step 1. Focus on classes that suggest interactivity — things with words like: hero, animate, scroll, slide, reveal, split, parallax, hover, active, open, toggle, frame, moon, star, video, zoom, marquee, cursor, loader, transition, etc.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 4 — Fetch the CSS\\r\\n\\r\\nUse WebFetch on the main CSS file. The goal is to extract the complete design system.\\r\\n\\r\\nAgain, build the prompt dynamically using actual class names from the HTML:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Base prompt:**\\r\\n```\\r\\nExtract the COMPLETE design system from this CSS file:\\r\\n\\r\\nInclude:\\r\\n- Every color value (hex, rgb, hsl, CSS custom properties)\\r\\n- Every @font-face declaration with font-family names, src paths, weights, and styles\\r\\n- Every font-family assignment and which elements use them\\r\\n- Every @keyframes animation with full keyframe definitions\\r\\n- Every CSS custom property (--variable) definition and usage\\r\\n- Every media query breakpoint and what changes at each\\r\\n- All transform and transition properties\\r\\n- All position:fixed and position:sticky elements\\r\\n- All z-index values and stacking context\\r\\n- The spacing/sizing system (padding, margin, gap patterns)\\r\\n- Border-radius values used across the site\\r\\n- Box-shadow and text-shadow values\\r\\n- Gradient definitions\\r\\n- Mix-blend-mode and filter usage\\r\\n- The responsive typography system (font sizes at different breakpoints)\\r\\n- Any CSS animations or transitions on pseudo-elements (::before, ::after)\\r\\n- Grid and flexbox layout patterns for major sections\\r\\n- Any mask-image or clip-path usage\\r\\n- The noise/grain/texture overlay implementation if present\\r\\n\\r\\nAlso extract all CSS rules for classes containing these terms:\\r\\n[INSERT COMMA-SEPARATED LIST OF KEY CLASS NAME STEMS FROM THE HTML]\\r\\n\\r\\nReturn actual CSS rules and values. Do not summarize — return as much raw CSS as possible.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReplace the class name placeholder with stems extracted from the HTML. For example, if the HTML has `.home-hero__moon-inner`, include `hero`, `moon`. If it has `.circle-slider__wheel`, include `circle`, `slider`, `wheel`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Step 5 — Assemble the Teardown Document\\r\\n\\r\\nCombine findings from all three sources (HTML, JS, CSS) into a structured markdown file.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Save to:** The user\'s working directory at `research/YYYY-MM-DD-{site-slug}-teardown.md`\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the working directory doesn\'t have a `research/` folder, save to the current directory instead.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Use this template:**\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\n# Site Teardown: {Site Name}\\r\\n\\r\\n**URL:** {url}\\r\\n**Built by:** {agency/developer if identifiable from meta tags or footer}\\r\\n**Platform:** {WordPress/Next.js/custom/etc — inferred from HTML structure}\\r\\n**Date analyzed:** {YYYY-MM-DD}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tech Stack (Confirmed from Source)\\r\\n\\r\\n| Technology | Evidence | Purpose |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| {library} | {where you saw it — script tag, code reference, etc.} | {what it does on this site} |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Design System\\r\\n\\r\\n### Colors\\r\\n| Name/Usage | Value |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| {Primary background} | {#hex} |\\r\\n\\r\\n### Typography\\r\\n| Role | Font Family | Weight | Letter-spacing | Sizes |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| {Headings} | {font name} | {weight} | {spacing} | {size range} |\\r\\n\\r\\nFont files: {list actual font file paths if found in @font-face}\\r\\n\\r\\n### Spacing System\\r\\n{Describe the spacing approach — CSS custom properties, fluid sizing with min()/clamp(), fixed values, etc.}\\r\\n\\r\\n### Responsive Approach\\r\\n{Media query strategy — breakpoints, orientation-based, container queries, etc.}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Effects Breakdown\\r\\n\\r\\n| Effect | Implementation | Complexity | Cloneable? |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| {effect name} | {how it works — 1-2 sentences} | {Low/Med/High} | {Yes/Partially/Hard} |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Implementation Details\\r\\n\\r\\n### {Most Impressive Effect #1}\\r\\n```\\r\\n{Detailed explanation of how it works}\\r\\n{Code snippets from the JS/CSS fetch}\\r\\n{The key insight that makes it work}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### {Most Impressive Effect #2}\\r\\n```\\r\\n{Same structure}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n{Repeat for each major effect worth detailing}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Assets Needed to Recreate\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **{Asset type}** — {description, suggested source (Midjourney prompt, stock site, generate with code, etc.)}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Build Plan\\r\\n\\r\\n### Recommended Stack\\r\\n- {Framework}: {why}\\r\\n- {Styling}: {why}\\r\\n- {Animation library}: {why}\\r\\n- {Other packages}: {why}\\r\\n\\r\\n### NPM Packages\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpm install {package1} {package2} {package3}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### Section-by-Section Build Order\\r\\n\\r\\n**Section 1: {name}**\\r\\n- {what it contains}\\r\\n- {key interactions/animations}\\r\\n- {implementation approach}\\r\\n\\r\\n**Section 2: {name}**\\r\\n- {same structure}\\r\\n\\r\\n{Continue for all sections}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes\\r\\n- {Any gotchas, premium plugins needed, licensing concerns}\\r\\n- {Things that can\'t be cloned easily and suggested alternatives}\\r\\n- {Performance considerations}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### Filling the Template — Guidance\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen assembling the teardown, keep these principles in mind:\\r\\n\\r\\n**Be specific, not vague.** \\"GSAP ScrollTrigger with scrub:true pinned to .hero, scaling from 1 to 0.3\\" is useful. \\"Uses scroll animations\\" is not. The person reading this needs to build it without seeing the original site.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Include the \\"reveal\\" for each effect.** The most valuable part of a teardown is showing that an impressive-looking effect has a simple implementation. If the moon illumination is just 54 images swapped on mousemove, say that clearly. If the parallax is just `translateX(mouseX / 50)`, include that formula.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Distinguish confirmed vs inferred.** If you found the exact GSAP config in the JS, say \\"confirmed from source.\\" If you\'re inferring from HTML class names and general patterns, say \\"inferred\\" so the builder knows what\'s solid and what might need adjustment.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Group effects by section.** The effects table gives the overview, but the implementation details should follow the page flow top-to-bottom so the builder can work section by section.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Be practical about assets.** Don\'t just say \\"needs images.\\" Say how many, what kind, suggest Midjourney prompts or stock photo search terms, and note if any can be generated with code (SVGs, noise textures, gradients).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Common Patterns to Watch For\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are the most common \\"impressive-looking but simple\\" patterns found on award-winning sites:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Image sequences on scroll/mouse** — Preloaded frames, swap visibility based on input. Looks like video/3D, it\'s just images.\\r\\n- **SplitText character reveals** — GSAP SplitText wraps each character in a span, then staggers their opacity/transform.\\r\\n- **Parallax layers** — Multiple elements moving at different speeds on mousemove or scroll. Just different multipliers on transform.\\r\\n- **Scroll-scrubbed animations** — GSAP ScrollTrigger with `scrub: true`. Ties any animation to scroll position.\\r\\n- **CSS custom property animations** — JS updates a `--progress` variable, CSS uses `calc()` with it. Clean separation of behavior and style.\\r\\n- **Smooth scrolling** — Lenis or Locomotive Scroll wrapping the page. Just a library init.\\r\\n- **Page transitions** — Barba.js or Swup. Overlay fades in, content swaps, overlay fades out.\\r\\n- **Noise/grain overlay** — A fixed div with a noise texture image and `mix-blend-mode: overlay`.\\r\\n- **Custom cursors** — GSAP `quickSetter` tracking mouse position on a fixed element.\\r\\n- **Marquee text** — CSS or GSAP infinite horizontal translation loop.\\r\\n- **Magnetic buttons** — Element subtly follows cursor when hovering nearby. Just mousemove + transform on the button.\\r\\n- **Reveal on scroll** — IntersectionObserver or ScrollTrigger adding a class, CSS handles the transition.\\r\\n"}'}]
