Triggers on \\\\\\"save X in my vocabulary\\\\\\", \\\\\\"add X to my dictionary\\\\\\", \\\\\\"X --> Y\\\\\\", \\\\\\"X should always be Y\\\\\\", \\\\\\"replace X with Y\\\\\\", a bare mis-heard word with an arrow, \\\\\\"make a mode for ...\\\\\\", \\\\\\"scan my transcripts\\\\\\", \\\\\\"what keeps getting messed up\\\\\\", \\\\\\"what should I add to Superwhisper\\\\\\", \\\\\\"restart Superwhisper\\\\\\", and any mention of Superwhisper, Super Whisper, or \\\\\\"the dictation\\\\\\" in the same breath as a word, a spelling, or a mode. Also use it when a dictated message from Joshua arrives with an obvious mis-hearing of a known term, even if he does not ask: note it and offer the fix. Covers the whole loop: edit the vault files, think through the likely mis-hearing variants, sync into the app, restart it silently, verify the load, and scan the transcript history for the next improvements.\\"\\r\\n+description: Maintain Joshua\'s Superwhisper dictation setup on this Windows laptop. Use for vocabulary requests, spoken correction mappings, custom modes, transcript-error scans, configuration checks, or restarting Superwhisper.\\r\\n ---\\r\\n \\r\\n # Superwhisper\\r\\n \\r\\n-Joshua dictates almost everything he sends you. Superwhisper is the app that does it, and the\\r\\n-`dev/ai-os/superwhisper/` folder is its source of truth. Your job is to keep that folder right\\r\\n-and get it into the app without him having to touch anything. He uses Left Alt to toggle recording; preserve it and verify it after restart.\\r\\n+The source of truth is `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/superwhisper/`. Read\\r\\n+`superwhisper/CLAUDE.md` before changing anything; despite its name, it contains the\\r\\n+app-specific operating rules for both runtimes.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Read `dev/ai-os/superwhisper/CLAUDE.md` first if this is a new session. It holds the rules\\r\\n-that cost a whole session to learn (the Claude Desktop sandbox redirects writes, the app\\r\\n-reads config only at launch, prompt examples cannot be written from outside the app). This\\r\\n-skill assumes those rules; it does not repeat them.\\r\\n+Joshua uses Left Alt to toggle recording. Preserve it and verify it after restart. Complete configuration changes, sync them, restart\\r\\n+the app silently, and verify what loaded without opening Superwhisper or Settings.\\r\\n \\r\\n-## The three requests\\r\\n+## Correction or vocabulary request\\r\\n \\r\\n-### 1. A word or a correction (\\"Firecrow --> Firecrawl\\", \\"save Pre-workout\\")\\r\\n+- `X --> Y`, \\"replace X with Y\\", and \\"X should always be Y\\" add rows to\\r\\n+  `superwhisper/replacements.md`. Matching is case-insensitive.\\r\\n+- \\"Save X in my vocabulary\\" adds X to `superwhisper/vocabulary.md` only when X is an\\r\\n+  unusual name or domain term the voice model needs help recognizing.\\r\\n+- Do not automatically add replacement targets to vocabulary. That experiment expanded\\r\\n+  the list to 654 entries on 2026-09-11 and immediately worsened transcription. Keep the\\r\\n+  vocabulary around 40 terms.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Decide which of two files it belongs in. They do different jobs:\\r\\n+For a replacement, add useful variants in the same pass: the exact error, a trailing-period\\r\\n+form for one-word dictation, plausible phonetic spellings, joined or split words, and spoken\\r\\n+punctuation such as \\"dot\\" or \\"at\\". Add only variants that plausibly identify the intended\\r\\n+term. Never replace an ordinary word such as `seven`, `white`, or `care` merely because it\\r\\n+resembles a name; that would corrupt normal sentences.\\r\\n \\r\\n-- `replacements.md` fixes a specific mis-hearing. Deterministic, case-insensitive, applied\\r\\n-  after transcription. This is where almost everything goes.\\r\\n-- `vocabulary.md` is a hint list sent to the voice model. Keep it around 40 entries: names and\\r\\n-  terms the model cannot know. On 2026-09-11 it was pushed to 654 (every replacement target\\r\\n-  auto-added) and transcription got noticeably worse within the hour, so that rule is gone;\\r\\n-  the sync sends exactly the listed terms. Add a vocabulary line only for a term the model\\r\\n-  keeps getting wrong.\\r\\n+Use `apply_patch` for vault edits. Preserve the Markdown table format:\\r\\n \\r\\n-A correction with an arrow is a replacement row only. \\"Save the word X\\" means a vocabulary\\r\\n-line (if the model really cannot know it) plus replacement rows for how it gets mangled.\\r\\n-\\r\\n-**Think through the variants before writing.** One row per spelling he sends is not enough;\\r\\n-he sends the next mis-hearing ten minutes later. For each term, write rows for:\\r\\n-\\r\\n-- the exact form he sent, lowercase on the heard side\\r\\n-- the same with a trailing period (`firecrow.`), because a one-word dictation gets\\r\\n-  auto-punctuated and the rule will not match with the period attached\\r\\n-- phonetic neighbours: swapped vowels (fisseha → faseya, fuseya, fasaya, fesaya), doubled\\r\\n-  or dropped letters (firecrawll, firecraw), split or joined words (pre workout, preworkout,\\r\\n-  cloudcode)\\r\\n-- spoken forms of anything with punctuation: \\"at ... dot com\\", \\"dot study\\"\\r\\n-- a c…854 tokens truncated…ode is not the active one, say so). Apply the safe rows in the same turn,\\r\\n-run the loop, and leave the judgment calls to him.\\r\\n-\\r\\n-## The loop\\r\\n-\\r\\n-Every change ends the same way. `sync.py --restart` does all of it:\\r\\n-\\r\\n-```bash\\r\\n-python C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/superwhisper/sync.py --restart\\r\\n+```powershell\\r\\n+python sync.py --restart\\r\\n ```\\r\\n \\r\\n-1. Reads `vocabulary.md`, `replacements.md`, `modes/*.md`.\\r\\n-2. Or let the agent create one for you.\\r\\n    required_for: all API requests\\r\\nmetadata: {\\"openclaw\\":{\\"emoji\\":\\"▶️\\",\\"requires\\":{\\"env\\":[\\"TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\"]},\\"primaryEnv\\":\\"TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\",\\"homepage\\":\\"https://transcriptapi.com\\"},\\"hermes\\":{\\"tags\\":[\\"youtube\\",\\"captions\\",\\"subtitles\\",\\"transcripts\\",\\"video\\",\\"accessibility\\",\\"translation\\"],\\"category\\":\\"media\\"}}\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Captions\\r\\n\\r\\nExtract closed captions from YouTube videos via [TranscriptAPI.com](https://transcriptapi.com).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Setup\\r\\n\\r\\nIf `$TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY` is not set, read [references/auth-setup.md](references/auth-setup.md) and follow the instructions there to get and store the key.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Required Headers\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery request needs two headers:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Authorization:[REDACTED] `Bearer $TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY`\\r\\n- **User-Agent:** your agent\'s name and version if known (e.g. `HermesAgent/0.11.0`, `ClaudeCode/1.0`). Version is optional — agent name alone is fine. Do not omit this header or send a bare default — Cloudflare will return a 403 (error code 1010) and block the request.\\r\\n\\r\\n## GET /api/v2/youtube/transcript\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ncurl -s \\"https://transcriptapi.com/api/v2/youtube/transcript\\\\\\r\\n?video_url=VIDEO_URL&format=json&include_timestamp=true&send_metadata=true\\" \\\\\\r\\n  -H \\"Authorization: [REDACTED] $TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\" \\\\\\r\\n  -H \\"User-Agent: YourAgent/1.0\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n| Param               | Required | Default | Values                              |\\r\\n| ------------------- | -------- | ------- | ----------------------------------- |\\r\\n| `video_url`         | yes      | —       | YouTube URL or video ID             |\\r\\n| `format`            | no       | `json`  | `json` (structured), `text` (plain) |\\r\\n| `include_timestamp` | no       | `true`  | `true`, `false`                     |\\r\\n| `send_metadata`     | no       | `false` | `true`, `false`                     |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Response** (`format=json` — best for accessibility/timing):\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"video_id\\": \\"dQw4w9WgXcQ\\",\\r\\n  \\"language\\": \\"en\\",\\r\\n  \\"transcript\\": [\\r\\n    { \\"text\\": \\"We\'re no strangers to love\\", \\"start\\": 18.0, \\"duration\\": 3.5 },\\r\\n    { \\"text\\": \\"You know the rules and so do I\\", \\"start\\": 21.5, \\"duration\\": 2.8 }\\r\\n  ],\\r\\n  \\"metadata\\": { \\"title\\": \\"...\\", \\"author_name\\": \\"...\\", \\"thumbnail_url\\": \\"...\\" }\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n- `start`: seconds from video start\\r\\n- `duration`: how long caption is displayed\\r\\n\\r\\n**Response** (`format=text` — readable):\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\"video_id\\": \\"dQw4w9WgXcQ\\",\\r\\n  \\"language\\": \\"en\\",\\r\\n  \\"transcript\\": \\"[00:00:18] We\'re no strangers to love\\\\n[00:00:21] You know the rules...\\"\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tips\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use `format=json` for sync\'d captions (accessibility tools, timing analysis).\\r\\n- Use `format=text` with `include_timestamp=false` for clean reading.\\r\\n- Auto-generated captions are available for most videos; manual CC is higher quality.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Errors\\r\\n\\r\\n| Code     | Meaning          | Action                                         |\\r\\n| -------- | ---------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| 401      | Bad API key      | Check key                                      |\\r\\n| 402      | No credits       | transcriptapi.com/billing                      |\\r\\n| 403/1010 | Cloudflare block | Add or fix User-Agent header                   |\\r\\n| 404      | No captions      | Video doesn\'t have CC enabled                  |\\r\\n| 408      | Timeout          | Retry once after 2s                            |\\r\\n\\r\\n1 credit per request. Free tier: 100 credits, 300 req/min.\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: session-handoff-nate\\r\\ndescription: Nate Herk\'s original upstream session-handoff, kept for reference. Use ONLY when Joshua explicitly asks for \\"Nate\'s handoff\\" or \\"the original handoff\\" — the customized `session-handoff` skill is the default and is a strict superset of this one.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Session Handoff (Nate Herk original)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduce a repeatable end-of-session summary so the user can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should be able to pick up by reading this summary alone.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When to invoke\\r\\n\\r\\nUser says: \\"session handoff\\", \\"wrap up session\\", \\"hand off\\", \\"handoff summary\\", \\"let\'s wrap up\\", \\"summarize before I clear\\", or any near-equivalent. Also invoke proactively if the user says they\'re about to `/clear` without having run it yet.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How to produce the summary\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.\\r\\n2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**\\r\\n   - Plan files referenced this session (check `C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\.claude\\\\plans\\\\` if a plan was mentioned).\\r\\n   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.\\r\\n   - Background processes you started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.\\r\\n   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don\'t grep to re-discover.\\r\\n   - Memory files written or updated (`C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\.claude\\\\projects\\\\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\\\\memory\\\\`).\\r\\n   - Unresolved questions — things you asked the user that never got a clear answer, or things the user asked that got deflected.\\r\\n3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn\'t touch it this session, it doesn\'t belong here.\\r\\n4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Where it started\\r\\n<2-3 sentences: what the user asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Decisions locked + what shipped\\r\\n- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>\\r\\n- ...\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key files for next session\\r\\n- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>\\r\\n- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)\\r\\n- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)\\r\\n\\r\\n## Running state\\r\\n- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or \\"none\\"\\r\\n- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or \\"none\\"\\r\\n- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or \\"none\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification — how to confirm things still work\\r\\n- `<command>` — <expected outcome>\\r\\n- ...\\r\\n\\r\\n## Deferred + open questions\\r\\n- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>\\r\\n- Open: <question needing the user\'s input> — <context>\\r\\n\\r\\n## Pick up here\\r\\n<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hard rules\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill.\\r\\n2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write \\"none\\" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.\\r\\n3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory.\\r\\n4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in \\"Key files\\" so the next agent reads it before anything else.\\r\\n5. **No emojis, no hype, no \\"great job\\" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Match the tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.\\r\\n6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in \\"Running state\\" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Anti-patterns — do not do these\\r\\n\\r\\n- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.\\r\\n- Listing files by relative path.\\r\\n- Skipping the \\"Running state\\" section because \\"nothing is running\\" — write \\"none\\" instead.\\r\\n- Writing the summary to `~/.claude/handoffs/` or any file. This is chat-only by design.\\r\\n- Adding a \\"what went well / what went poorly\\" retrospective. This isn\'t a retro.\\r\\n- Recommending next steps beyond the single \\"Pick up here\\" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n*Upstream original by Nate Herk, installed 2026-09-07 for reference. text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/audits/skills-2026-10-09/SUMMARY.md\n+# Repair routing and stale instructions before removing useful skills\n+\n+Audit started 2026-10-09; resumed 2026-10-10. Completion and exact coverage are recorded in `coverage.json` and `ALL-SKILL-DISPOSITIONS.md`. This is a specialist skill-library audit, not a Four Cs score or evidence every workflow ran successfully.\n+\n+## Recommendation\n+\n+Keep the useful capabilities. Each disposition retains its exact path and scope in the complete ledger.\n+\n+## Repair in this order\n+\n+| Priority | Skill or route | Confirmed instruction problem | Concrete repair and check |\n+|---|---|---|---|\n+| 1 | Global `claude-api`, exposed as `Codex-api` | Claude identity was mechanically replaced with Codex while the instructions still require the Anthropic SDK. Its trigger also catches generic language and persona-agent tasks. | Restore Anthropic/Claude identity; trigger only Anthropic API implementation. Persona creation must route to `persona-agent-creator`. Preserve the healthy catalog API reference. |\n+| 1 | Global `academy-guide` | Anthropic Academy URLs and course identities were changed into `academy.Codex.com` and Codex product names. | Restore source identities and URLs; narrow to an explicitly relevant academy-learning request. |\n+| 1 | `lsatu-cc-handoff` | Operational briefs are written/moved under immutable `raw/sources`; its generated brief defaults to direct main commits and push after each step. | Use `handoffs/` for operational briefs, preserve sources, and carry the actual branch/commit/push authorization from the task. |\n+| 1 | `chat-export` | Promises a full verbatim conversation while reconstructing the currently visible context. Compaction can omit earlier turns. | Retrieve the actual transcript/log or explicitly label a visible-context export and missing ranges. Never fabricate vanished turns. |\n+| 2 | `code-review` and `requesting-code-review` | Commit-range comparisons omit dirty work during a work-in-progress review. | Inventory and include requested committed, staged, unstaged and untracked changes. Record the exact reviewed scope. The isolated fixture below proves this Git limitation. |\n+| 2 | `bootcamp-parity-audit` | Three mandatory benchmark anchors were absent at both literal legacy and current-root equivalents. | Resolve existing authoritative benchmarks before asserting parity; do not invent replacements. |\n+| 2 | `guided-drill-assignment` | Transcript inspection/recommendation triggers lead into an assignment workflow and compete with the current assignment executor. | Separate inspect, recommend and assign modes. A transcript-only request must make no live write. Keep lesson-selection knowledge. |\n+| 2 | Consultation draft skills | Existing unsent draft reuse is not explicitly checked by stable event/thread/recipient key. | Reuse the matching draft; a repeated sweep should not create another. No duplicate live draft was created in this audit. |\n+| 2 | `qa` | General QA trigger instructs immediate GitHub issue publication without review. | Distinguish report-only QA from explicitly authorized issue filing. |\n+| 2 | `test-driven-development` | Commands deleting/restarting existing implementation solely because a failing test was not written first. | Preserve user work; use meaningful red-green regression tests proportionately. Never delete existing work as a ceremony. |\n+| 2 | `resolving-merge-conflicts`, `setup-pre-commit` | Stage-all/automatic commit defaults risk including unrelated work. Hook setup also lacks preserve/merge guidance for existing hook configuration. | Capture baseline, stage only owned paths, preserve existing hooks and honor explicit commit scope. |\n+| 2 | `youtube-full`, `watch`, inactive transcript aliases | Broad YouTube triggers select keyed/credit-consuming services; watch adds another secret home and cleanup deletion. | Free existing caption collector first, explicit optional paid fallback and bounded authorization, central secret route, preserve captures until removal authorized. |\n+| 2 | Codex `lovable-prompt` | Requires Supabase MCP tools absent from this session. | Route through an available approved transport. Mandatory Lovable deployment applies to Edge Functions, not a blanket ban on direct SQL. Verify app-specific safe-push route separately. |\n+| 2 | Codex catalog `imagegen` | Chromakey guidance assumes built-in transparency is unavailable, but the current exposed tool has `transparent_background`. | Use the actual built-in parameter and inspect output. The current global system skill already has the correct capability; the catalog copy is stale. |\n+| 3 | `decision-mapping`, Codex `research-deep` | Misspelled `domain-modelling` route and an absent hardcoded research validator path. | Resolve exact installed dependencies before invocation; use the skill's actual support directory. |\n+| 3 | Literal Claude `Skill` calls in Codex copies | No native Skill invocation tool is exposed in this Codex session. | Read the appropriate skill entry point or use the supported host route. Preserve vendor sources and maintain a small project overlay. |\n+| 3 | Gemini legacy audit/Claudex/Kling copies | Mac paths, launchctl, old host roles and older Kling contract survive outside the validator's Claude/Codex scope. | Reconcile deliberate host differences and current shared runner/version; add Gemini validation. |\n+| 3 | High-end design presets | Generic triggers impose nested cards while another design skill says nested cards are always wrong. Other redesign instructions fabricate organic data/dates. | Make aesthetics explicit optional presets; preserve the project's design system, facts and accessibility. |\n+| 3 | `webapp-testing`, `web-artifacts-builder`, `teach` | Universal networkidle is not UI readiness; artifact builds default into cwd and discourage testing; learning workspace defaults can clutter the vault. | Select sibling application/learning homes, assert actual readiness and run relevant checks before completion. |\n+| 3 | `setup-matt-pocock-skills`, `setup-ts-deep-modules` | Select one instruction file when this project requires shared rules in both manuals. | Host/project overlay must mirror shared pointers without regenerating hand-owned manuals. |\n+\n+## Cleanup candidates, preserving information\n+\n+| Candidate | Recommended disposition | Why |\n+|---|---|---|\n+| `obsidian-vault` for this AI OS | Leave inactive/deactivate its current-vault trigger | Targets `/mnt/d/Obsidian Vault/AI Research` and a flat no-folder layout, conflicting with this structured vault. Current ingest/query/lint own the work. |\n+| `lsat-u-topic-builder` | Retire broad entry after preserving useful references | `bootcamp-builder` explicitly supersedes it; old Mac search and unsafe source/duplicate heuristics remain. |\n+| `example-command`, `example-skill` | Reference-only | Educational samples have value but should not compete with real creator workflows. |\n+| `brand-guidelines` | Explicit Anthropic-brand only | Its palette is Anthropic's, not Joshua's. |\n+| `using-superpowers` | Narrow or explicit invocation | Catch-all every-conversation loading adds routing and approval friction. |\n+| `high-end-visual-design` | Explicit aesthetic preset | A valid style choice should not override every frontend brief. |\n+| `to-issues` and `to-tickets` | Consolidation candidate | Share vertical-slice breakdown; to-tickets adds local tracker/native edges and expand-contract. Preserve compatibility and approval semantics. |\n+| `writing-beats` and `writing-shape` | Consolidation candidate | Both shape a fixed source pile collaboratively; beat mode can share one authoring core. Keep `writing-fragments` as the separate exploration phase. |\n+| `writing-great-skills` and `writing-for-agents` | Shared reference candidate | Repeated vocabulary and hierarchy guidance can live once, with skill-specific mechanics retained. |\n+| `ubiquitous-language` and domain modeling glossary routes | Consolidation candidate | Avoid competing `UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md` and `GLOSSARY.md` truths; preserve explicit ambiguity and relationship analysis. |\n+| Inactive YouTube aliases | Keep inactive, optional provider reference | They offer overlapping keyed TranscriptAPI routes. Do not reactivate a dozen broad names; preserve any unique search/channel/playlist references. |\n+| Original Nate handoff copies | Keep historical reference | Customized current handoff supersedes them. Their broad body triggers conflict with the frontmatter's explicit-only boundary if reactivated. |\n+\n+No usage telemetry was gathered. An unfamiliar specialized skill is not proven useless. There is no recommendation to blanket-delete GWS helpers, media tools, runtime copies or specialist references.\n+\n+## Keep these core boundaries\n+\n+- `ingest`: add a new source and compile knowledge.\n+- `second-brain-query`: answer from saved knowledge with evidence and freshness escalation.\n+- `second-brain-lint`: check knowledge structure, contradictions, stale claims and missing pages.\n+- `audit`: system health; `skill-lint`: package checks; `skill-doctor`: actual usage evidence. These do different jobs.\n+- `persona-agent-creator`: build another documented-method agent; `karpathy-teach`: use the existing reviewed teaching agent.\n+- Claudex provider pairs and compatibility wrappers: deliberate runtime directions in `PARITY.md`, not accidental duplicates.\n+- GWS domain/helper skills: API surfaces and specific actions, not dozens of copies of one workflow.\n+\n+## Trigger counterexamples to use during repairs\n+\n+These are static instruction comparisons, **not observed native selector execution**.\n+\n+| Request | Correct intent | Current collision | Acceptance requirement |\n+|---|---|---|---|\n+| Create a persona agent for this author | Persona factory | Global Codex-api catches unstated-provider agent tasks | Factory route; no API/provider assumption |\n+| Read this student's transcript and tell me where they struggle | Inspection | Guided-drill assignment continues into execution | No assignment/send/live write |\n+| Give me the transcript of this YouTube URL | Free caption extraction first | Broad keyed/credit routes | Keyless collector or explicit limitation; paid fallback only authorized |\n+| Review my uncommitted changes | Worktree review | Three-dot HEAD range | Includes actual dirty/staged/untracked scope |\n+| Polish this UI using our current design system | Preserve existing visual language | Presets impose contradictory universal aesthetics | Follow actual design system; motion/accessibility preserved |\n+| Improve this small approved configuration | Direct authorized scoped edit | Generic brainstorming imposes new approval ceremony | Existing authorization honored |\n+| What do my saved notes say about this? | Query | Generic old Obsidian workflow | Current schema/index retrieval, no alternate vault |\n+\n+## What was checked and what was not\n+\n+Full semantic entry-point reads were recovered after the original concatenated output truncated. Exact byte copies inherit a fully reviewed base; nonidentical variants require reviewed complete differences and a fully reviewed base. SHA-256 receipts identify physical files. LF-normalized exact text is explicitly distinguished from byte identity. Fresh hash reconciliation prevents stale review receipts from being silently counted.\n+\n+The full per-entry table records scope and recommended disposition. Historical snapshots are inventoried and preserved; unmatched inactive entry points are reviewed separately. Supporting scripts, vendor payloads, assets and external plugin cache trees were not exhaustively executed. Their presence and selected routes were checked where relevant. This is not a claim every file on the machine was read or every third-party skill installed in every possible plugin root was tested.\n+\n+The existing validator checks project activated/catalog drift and Claude/Codex parity; it does not certify owned global body correctness or Gemini. Add bounded owned-global/provider sanity and Gemini checks rather than mirroring every external vendor package into this repo.\n+\n+## AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md\n+\n+Both current root manuals were read in full. Shared routing and guardrails agree in the inspected text; runtime identity differences are deliberate. They define the canonical root, immutable source evidence, outside-vault application homes, dual-runtime copies, explicit deletion permission and live-system authority. No manual changes were needed for this audit. Legacy skill instructions should adapt to those authorities. The old session-handoff F26 drift is resolved and is not a current defect.\n+\n+## Safe behavioral evidence\n+\n+The parent ran an isolated temporary Git repository: commit one file, then edit it without committing. Predicted the committed three-dot range would miss the edit while worktree diff would show it. Observed `git diff HEAD...HEAD` was empty; `git diff` contained `+changed`. This demonstrates the documented review scope defect. The proposed correction is to add explicit worktree/staged/untracked inventory, not change the production skills during an audit. The temporary fixture was cleaned by its isolated TemporaryDirectory lifecycle; no AI OS commit or mutation occurred.\n+\n+The parent also ran `gws gmail +send --help`, which explicitly supports `--draft` and `--dry-run`. The booking draft flag is valid. No message or draft was sent/created.\n+\n+## Seven-rule grades\n+\n+Based on the [reviewed method](../../wiki/karpathy/rules.md), not a claim about Karpathy's endorsement.\n+\n+| Rule | Grade | Concrete evidence |\n+|---|---|---|\n+| Mechanism reconstruction | Pass | Trace request, trigger, selected route and resulting side effect; show inspection vs assignment counterexample. |\n+| Small baseline | Pass | One file with one uncommitted edit isolates commit-range review scope. |\n+| Prediction/run/comparison | Pass | Parent predicted empty committed range vs visible worktree diff, then observed both. |\n+| Counterexample/fix | Pass for diagnostic proposal | Fixture exposes the omission and worktree comparison corrects the diagnostic coverage. Production instruction repair is intentionally not applied. |\n+| Relevant execution | Pass within audit scope | Safe Git fixture, actual CLI help, filesystem/hash/coverage checks. No claim live skill workflows ran. |\n+| Explicit assumptions | Pass | Static routing is separated from native selection; telemetry, payload and external cache limits explicit. |\n+| Justified simplicity | Pass | Consolidate repeated entry routes while preserving specialist knowledge and deliberate runtime differences. |\n+\n+## What I ran\n+\n+Catalog/global entry inventory, full semantic body and complete variant reviews, hashes and coverage reconciliation, selected support/route probes, current manuals/PARITY checks, parent isolated Git fixture and read-only GWS help.\n+\n+## What came out\n+\n+Ranked repair and cleanup recommendations, exact per-entry dispositions and scope evidence. The main risk is stale or overbroad instructions, not simply the number of files.\n+\n+## What I changed\n+\n+Audit reports and evidence ledgers only. No skill was deleted, deactivated, rewritten, committed or published; no live data or configuration was changed by this audit.\n*** End Patch"));

If missing, run `gws generate-skills` to create it.\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ngws gmail <resource> <method> [flags]\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Helper Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n| Command | Description |\\r\\n|---------|-------------|\\r\\n| [`+send`](../../../skills/codex/gws-gmail-send/SKILL.md) | Send an email |\\r\\n| [`+triage`](../gws-gmail-triage/SKILL.md) | Show unread inbox summary (sender, subject, date) |\\r\\n| [`+reply`](../../../skills/codex/gws-gmail-reply/SKILL.md) | Reply to a message (handles threading automatically) |\\r\\n| [`+reply-all`](../../../skills/codex/gws-gmail-reply-all/SKILL.md) | Reply-all to a message (handles threading automatically) |\\r\\n| [`+forward`](../../../skills/codex/gws-gmail-forward/SKILL.md) | Forward a message to new recipients |\\r\\n| [`+read`](../gws-gmail-read/SKILL.md) | Read a message and extract its body or headers |\\r\\n| [`+watch`](../../../skills/codex/gws-gmail-watch/SKILL.md) | Watch for new emails and stream them as NDJSON |\\r\\n\\r\\n## API Resources\\r\\n\\r\\n### users\\r\\n\\r\\n  - `getProfile` — Gets the current user\'s Gmail profile.\\r\\n  - `stop` — Turn off push notification delivery for the given user mailbox. For more information, see [Configure push notifications in Gmail API](https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/guides/push).\\r\\n  - `watch` — Set up or update a push notification watch on the given user mailbox. For more information, see [Configure push notifications in Gmail API](https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/guides/push).\\r\\n  - `drafts` — Operations on the \'drafts\' resource\\r\\n  - `history` — Operations on the \'history\' resource\\r\\n  - `labels` — Operations on the \'labels\' resource\\r\\n  - `messages` — Operations on the \'messages\' resource\\r\\n  - `settings` — Operations on the \'settings\' resource\\r\\n  - `threads` — Operations on the \'threads\' resource\\r\\n\\r\\n## Discovering Commands\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore calling any API method, inspect it:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\n# Browse resources and methods\\r\\ngws gmail --help\\r\\n\\r\\n# Inspect a method\'s required params, types, and defaults\\r\\ngws schema gmail.<resource>.<method>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse `gws schema` output to build your `--params` and `--json` flags.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

Simulated trigger conflict and instruction analysis do not establish native skill-selector precedence or end-to-end execution.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Strongest additional findings\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Provider substitution is systemic.** The global claude-api 572 line body is exactly the canonical Claude body after case-insensitive `Claude Code`→`Codex`, then `Claude`→`Codex`. 104 lines change. Keep the underlying useful capability and restore provider/host distinctions.\\r\\n- **Global platform actions lag the project route.** The 75 line global body uses old browser-only operations and teacher?ws URLs, while current canonical body routes to headless Node/MCP with relevant verification. Preserve this current system capability; no API model availability claims were independently tested.\\r\\n- **Global chat-export promises full verbatim conversation reconstruction from current context** and uses old Mac/Documents destinations. Use available logs or disclose compacted/missing sections; keep current handoffs home.\\r\\n- **Browser harness domain-skills opt-in includes default public PR contribution** and remote paid sessions. These require actual task-scoped authorization; local CDP capability remains useful.\\r\\n- **System skill-audit is worth preserving**: it separates inspection from measured performance and changes, refuses usage inference from mentions, and labels discovery versus semantic coverage. ## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.4 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"ac2635","wall_time_seconds":0.2464413,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4189,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: Karpathy reviewed evidence hot page\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-08\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-09\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\ntags: [karpathy, llm-wiki]\\r\\nsources: [../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md, ../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md, ../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md]\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teach with small mechanisms and inspectable checks\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [collected sources are compiled](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). [Seven rules](rules.md) have exact quotes from two distinct original artifacts each. They are practical synthesis, with historical exceptions retained. Joshua\'s instructions and teaching voice remain authoritative.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Reconstruct the mechanism.** “Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating”. [Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Begin with a small baseline.** “get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.” [Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Predict, run and compare.** “make concrete hypotheses about what will happen and then either validate them with an experiment or investigate until we find some issue.” [Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Repair a cause through a counterexample.** “The problem is on line 291.” [Backprop article](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), the clipped-error gradient example.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Verify relevant behavior.** “Get it to write tests first and then pass them.” [Coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md). Running code alone does not establish correctness; [rules](rules.md) preserves a contrasting historical beam-search admission.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Expose assumptions and uncertainty.** “the models make wrong assumptions on your behalf and just run along with them without checking.” [Coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md). “with double digit percent uncertainty” [Animals vs Ghosts](../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Earn complexity.** “I cannot simplify this any further.” [Microgpt](../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md). Necessary production safeguards still belong in production.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe configured teaching agent reads this page first, uses at most five wiki pages, cites source pages and applies all seven rules. The skill grades its answer before presentation. End with **What I ran**, **What came out**, **What I changed**. Teaching code belongs outside the vault in `ai-os-projects/karpathy-method-tools/examples/`.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected Codex for this method. The fresh [tokenizer lesson](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/CODEX-TOKENIZER-ACCEPTANCE.md) passed seven tests with current-file receipts. The [Codex hook](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/CODEX-STOP-ACCEPTANCE.md) passed 36 tests and its six definitions are Trusted/enabled. Native main Stop blocked a scoped edit, recovered after execution and left an unrelated turn alone. Child SubagentStop is not separately live-tested. The [fresh native lesson](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/NATIVE-CODEX-LESSON-ACCEPTANCE.md) verified registered-agent discovery, real handoff and independent parent grading. Use $karpathy-teach in Codex; its parser rejects the original slash spelling. Generic fallback is no longer permitted by the skill. Claude quota is historical.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse existing `ingest` for new sources. Preserve raw evidence. Foreign authors, payloads, caption errors and linked media retain their limits. [Index](index.md), [principles](principles.md), [methods](methods.md), [current status](../../research/karpathy-video-2026-10-08/VERBATIM-COMPLIANCE.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: Seven evidence-supported Karpathy building and teaching rules\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-08\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-08\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\ntags: [karpathy, teaching, evidence]\\r\\nsources: [../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md, ../sources/karpathy/blog/calculator.md, ../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md, ../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md, ../sources/karpathy/lectures/vmj-3s1tku0.md, ../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md, ../sources/karpathy/lectures/l8prsuu81pu.md]\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Seven strongest rules for building and teaching\\r\\n\\r\\nFinalized 2026-10-08 after [the collected-source compilation audit](../../research/karpathy-brain-2026-10-08/COMPILATION-AUDIT.md). These seven recurring patterns are a synthesis of reviewed public evidence, not Karpathy\'s endorsement, private mind or a claim that every historical implementation followed them. Each has two distinct original source artifacts with exact quotation support. Separate artifacts corroborate recurring advice; they do not establish independent experimental replication. Automatic-caption wording remains uncorrected and is not audio-verified.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence** below describes his advice or demonstration. **Application** is our practical implementation and grading standard. Repository ownership, model-generated prompts, third-party contributions and syndicated copies were not used as sole proof of personal authorship. Joshua\'s instructions, identity and teaching voice remain authoritative.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Explain and reconstruct the mechanism\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** His study guidance distinguishes active reconstruction from passive understanding; his backprop article recommends implementation to solidify mechanism-level understanding.\\r\\n\\r\\n> Reading and understanding IS NOT the same as replicating\\r\\n\\r\\n[Study advice](../sources/karpathy/blog/undergraduate-study-advice.md), study section; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/other-first-party/cs.stanford.edu_people_karpathy_advice.html.md). Publication date unknown.\\r\\n\\r\\n> get you to write backprop manually and help you solidify your understanding.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Yes you should understand backprop](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), conclusion, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** Explain input, transformation and output using a small example, then invite the learner to reproduce or explain one step. Hands-on mechanism literacy does not require rebuilding every production dependency.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Start with one correct small case and a baseline\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** His recipe recommends getting a specific implementation working before generalizing. In Makemore he starts the neural pipeline with one word rather than the full dataset.\\r\\n\\r\\n> get that to work, and then generalize it later making sure that I get the same result.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “generalize a special case,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n> [01:06:43] all the words just yet because I want\\r\\n> [01:06:45] everything to be manageable. Uh so,\\r\\n> [01:06:47] let\'s just do the first word, which is\\r\\n\\r\\n[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). The preceding cue says not to take all words yet.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** Establish one small end-to-end case and a baseline, then introduce one justified change at a time. A teaching toy and a production system have different success requirements. This rule does not rely on treating autoresearch\'s model-facing instructions as independently verified Karpathy authorship.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Predict, run and compare\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** His recipe describes hypotheses followed by validation or investigation; Makemore predicts normalized row sums and unchanged results before running the vectorized calculation.\\r\\n\\r\\n> make concrete hypotheses about what will happen and then either validate them with an experiment or investigate until we find some issue.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Recipe](../sources/karpathy/blog/recipe.md), “The recipe,” 2019-04-25; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2019_04_25_recipe.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n> [00:44:04] taking its sum. We expect this to be\\r\\n> [00:44:06] one.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Makemore lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/pacmpygffxo.md), normalization check, 2022-09-07; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/PaCmpygFfXo.md). At 00:44:16 he runs the calculation; 00:44:19 reports the same result.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** State the expected behavior before executing a meaningful check, report what happened, and explain any mismatch. Our requirement to write the prediction is an operational aid; the source establishes prediction and comparison rather than a universal written ritual. Expected output never substitutes for observed output.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Expose a counterexample and repair its cause\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** Micrograd exposes a repeated-node derivative bug with a tiny counterexample; the backprop article traces why clipping a forward error accidentally eliminates the needed gradient.\\r\\n\\r\\n> [01:22:39] so here\'s the simplest case that shows\\r\\n> [01:22:42] the bug\\r\\n\\r\\n[Micrograd lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/vmj-3s1tku0.md), repeated-node example, 2022-08-16; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/VMj-3S1tku0.md). It expects derivative two, exposes overwritten contributions and repairs them with accumulation.\\r\\n\\r\\n> The problem is on line 291.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Backprop article](../sources/karpathy/blog/understand-backprop.md), DQN clipping example, 2016-12-19; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/medium/karpathy.medium.com_yes-you-should-understand-backprop-e2f06eab496b.md). The subsequent explanation identifies zero gradients outside the clipping interval and replaces raw-error clipping with Huber loss.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** Use a meaningful edge or failing case to make a cause inspectable, repair it and check the repaired behavior. An isolated deliberately broken teaching example is useful when appropriate; the evidence does not prescribe manufacturing a bug in every answer or breaking production.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Verify the relevant behavior, not merely a successful run\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** His coding notes recommend tests with explicit success criteria. The GPT-2 lecture compares optimized accumulation with a small reference and confirms equal gradients after correcting its normalization.\\r\\n\\r\\n> Get it to write tests first and then pass them.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Claude coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md), 2026-01-26, post `2015883857489522876`; [raw ledger](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl), selected own-author `text` field for that ID.\\r\\n\\r\\n> [02:43:32] that the U gradients are now identical\\r\\n\\r\\n[GPT-2 reproduction lecture](../sources/karpathy/lectures/l8prsuu81pu.md), reference comparison, 2024-06-09; [exact raw captions](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/youtube/transcripts/l8pRSuU81PU.md). The caption\'s “U” is preserved, not silently corrected.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** Run checks that test the requested behavior against a reference, invariant or expected outcome. Report commands, results and untested limits. A failed actual run still ran but cannot justify “works.” Local checks do not certify live, production or device acceptance.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Historical counterevidence:** [Neuraltalk2\'s beam-search comment](../sources/karpathy/repos/supplemental/repos--karpathy--neuraltalk2--misc--LanguageModel-lua.md) says it is not fully unit-tested and accepts plausible outputs; [runq.c](../sources/karpathy/repos/final-three/repos--karpathy--llama2-c--runq-c.md) explicitly calls chat an unsafe proof of concept. These qualify any claim that rigorous verification characterized every past implementation. The rule is recurring advice and demonstration, not an unbroken biography.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Make assumptions and uncertainty visible\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** His coding notes criticize unchecked assumptions. Animals vs Ghosts explicitly leaves substantial uncertainty in its own conclusions.\\r\\n\\r\\n> the models make wrong assumptions on your behalf and just run along with them without checking.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Claude coding notes](../sources/karpathy/x/claude-coding-notes.md), 2026-01-26, post `2015883857489522876`; [raw ledger](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/x/posts.jsonl), selected own-author `text` field.\\r\\n\\r\\n> with double digit percent uncertainty\\r\\n\\r\\n[Animals vs Ghosts](../sources/karpathy/animals-vs-ghosts.md), his own “As for my take” discussion, 2025-10-01; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/bearblog/karpathy.bearblog.dev_animals-vs-ghosts.md). This is his qualification, not the opening exposition of Sutton\'s view.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** Expose material assumptions, contradictions and uncertainty; resolve what the available evidence can resolve, then ask about consequential remaining ambiguity. Keep dated capability claims attributed. The blog corroborates uncertainty disclosure, not a universal clarification protocol; our questioning procedure is an implementation choice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Keep only complexity that earns its place\\r\\n\\r\\n**Evidence:** Microgpt pursues a minimal algorithmic teaching core; Calculator praises low dependency and operating burden. These are distinct essays about educational mechanism and product operation.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I cannot simplify this any further.\\r\\n\\r\\n[Microgpt](../sources/karpathy/blog/microgpt.md), opening, 2026-02-12; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/github-blog/karpathy.github.io_2026_02_12_microgpt.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n> almost zero \\"dependency footprint\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n[I love calculator](../sources/karpathy/blog/calculator.md), self-contained artifact discussion, 2024-09-08; [exact raw text](../../raw/sources/karpathy-brain/raw/blog/md/bearblog/karpathy.bearblog.dev_i-love-calculator.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Application:** Prefer a readable implementation with only the abstractions, dependencies and services the task needs. Preserve correctness while simplifying. Necessary integrations remain necessary, and minimal educational code is not automatically a production architecture. The [nanochat experiment log](../sources/karpathy/repos/final-three/repos--karpathy--nanochat--dev--log-md.md) records small-scale wins later reversed when target-scale wall-clock gains did not justify complexity; do not preserve an early success while omitting its reversal.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Quote and independence audit\\r\\n\\r\\nOn 2026-10-08 every displayed quotation above was checked as an exact substring of its linked raw artifact, including timestamped caption cues. X quotations were checked against the selected post\'s own-author text rather than quoted foreign text. Exact byte copies inherit a fully reviewed base; nonidentical variants require reviewed complete differences and a fully reviewed base. SHA-256 receipts identify physical files. LF-normalized exact text is explicitly distinguished from byte identity. Fresh hash reconciliation prevents stale review receipts from being silently counted.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe full per-entry table records scope and recommended disposition. Historical snapshots are inventoried and preserved; unmatched inactive entry points are reviewed separately. Supporting scripts, vendor payloads, assets and external plugin cache trees were not exhaustively executed. Their presence and selected routes were checked where relevant. This is not a claim every file on the machine was read or every third-party skill installed in every possible plugin root was tested.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe existing validator checks project activated/catalog drift and Claude/Codex parity; it does not certify owned global body correctness or Gemini. Add bounded owned-global/provider sanity and Gemini checks rather than mirroring every external vendor package into this repo.\\r\\n\\r\\n## AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md\\r\\n\\r\\nBoth current root manuals were read in full. Shared routing and guardrails agree in the inspected text; runtime identity differences are deliberate. They define the canonical root, immutable source evidence, outside-vault application homes, dual-runtime copies, explicit deletion permission and live-system authority. No manual changes were needed for this audit. Legacy skill instructions should adapt to those authorities. The old session-handoff F26 drift is resolved and is not a current defect.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Safe behavioral evidence\\r\\n\\r\\nThe parent ran an isolated temporary Git repository: commit one file, then edit it without committing. Predicted the committed three-dot range would miss the edit while worktree diff would show it. Observed `git diff HEAD...HEAD` was empty; `git diff` contained `+changed`. This demonstrates the documented review scope defect. The proposed correction is to add explicit worktree/staged/untracked inventory, not change the production skills during an audit. The temporary fixture was cleaned by its isolated TemporaryDirectory lifecycle; no AI OS commit or mutation occurred.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe parent also ran `gws gmail +send --help`, which explicitly supports `--draft` and `--dry-run`. The booking draft flag is valid. No message or draft was sent/created.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Seven-rule grades\\r\\n\\r\\nBased on the [reviewed method](../../wiki/karpathy/rules.md), not a claim about Karpathy\'s endorsement.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Rule | Grade | Concrete evidence |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Mechanism reconstruction | Pass | Trace request, trigger, selected route and resulting side effect; show inspection vs assignment counterexample. |\\r\\n| Small baseline | Pass | One file with one uncommitted edit isolates commit-range review scope. |\\r\\n| Prediction/run/comparison | Pass | Parent predicted empty committed range vs visible worktree diff, then observed both. |\\r\\n| Counterexample/fix | Pass for diagnostic proposal | Fixture exposes the omission and worktree comparison corrects the diagnostic coverage. Production instruction repair is intentionally not applied. |\\r\\n| Relevant execution | Pass within audit scope | Safe Git fixture, actual CLI help, filesystem/hash/coverage checks. No claim live skill workflows ran. |\\r\\n| Explicit assumptions | Pass | Static routing is separated from native selection; telemetry, payload and external cache limits explicit. |\\r\\n| Justified simplicity | Pass | Consolidate repeated entry routes while preserving specialist knowledge and deliberate runtime differences. |\\r\\n\\r\\n## What I ran\\r\\n\\r\\nCatalog/global entry inventory, full semantic body and complete variant reviews, hashes and coverage reconciliation, selected support/route probes, current manuals/PARITY checks, parent isolated Git fixture and read-only GWS help.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What came out\\r\\n\\r\\nRanked repair and cleanup recommendations, exact per-entry dispositions and scope evidence. The main risk is stale or overbroad instructions, not simply the number of files.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What I changed\\r\\n\\r\\nAudit reports and evidence ledgers only. No skill was deleted, deactivated, rewritten, committed or published; no live data or configuration was changed by this audit.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

For the loop, the host builds by default and the other provider inspects; the build compatibility entry point separately selects the other provider as builder.\\r\\n \\r\\n # Claudex Route\\r\\n \\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nFILE frontend-design\\r\\nBASE C:\\\\Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\skills\\\\claude\\\\frontend-design\\\\SKILL.md\\r\\n--- \\r\\n+++ \\r\\n@@ -1,71 +1,42 @@\\r\\n ---\\r\\n name: frontend-design\\r\\n-description: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don\'t read as templated defaults.\\r\\n+description: Create distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces with high design quality. Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, artifacts, posters, or applications (examples include websites, landing pages, dashboards, React components, HTML/CSS layouts, or when styling/beautifying any web UI). Generates creative, polished code and UI design that avoids generic AI aesthetics.\\r\\n license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt\\r\\n ---\\r\\n \\r\\n-# Frontend Design\\r\\n+This skill guides creation of distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces that avoid generic \\"AI slop\\" aesthetics. Implement real working code with exceptional attention to aesthetic details and creative choices.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Approach this as the design lead at a design studio known for giving every client a distinct visual identity that is not mistaken for anyone else\'s. This client has already rejected proposals that felt cliché or templated, and is paying for a distinctive point of view: make deliberate, opinionated choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this brief, and take aesthetic risk if justified.\\r\\n+The user provides frontend requirements: a component, page, application, or interface to build. They may include context about the purpose, audience, or technical constraints.\\r\\n \\r\\n-## Ground your designs in the subject matter\\r\\n+## Design Thinking\\r\\n \\r\\n-If the brief does not identify what the product or subject matter is, identify it yourself before designing, and confirm with the client. You can come up with one concrete subject, the design\'s audience, and the design\'s primary job, as a proposal. If there\'s any information in your memory about the client\'s preferences or context about what they\'re building, use that as a hint. The subject\'s industry, subject matter, materials, and vernacular are where distinctive visual choices come from — a design for a toy for girls aged 8–11 will be very aesthetically different from a dashboard for financial analysts. Build with the brief\'s real content and subject matter throughout.\\r\\n+Before coding, understand the context and commit to a BOLD aesthetic direction:\\r\\n+- **Purpose**: What problem does this interface solve? Who uses it?\\r\\n+- **Tone**: Pick an extreme: brutally minimal, maximalist chaos, retro-futuristic, organic/natural, luxury/refined, playful/toy-like, editorial/magazine, brutalist/raw, art deco/geometric, soft/pastel, industrial/utilitarian, etc. There are so many flavors to choose from. Use these for inspiration but design one that is true to the aesthetic direction.\\r\\n+- **Constraints**: Technical requirements (framework, performance, accessibility).\\r\\n+- **Differentiation**: What makes this UNFORGETTABLE? What\'s the one thing someone will remember?\\r\\n \\r\\n-## Design principles\\r\\n+**CRITICAL**: Choose a clear conceptual direction and execute it with precision. Bold maximalism and refined minimalism both work - the key is intentionality, not intensity.\\r\\n \\r\\n-For web designs, the hero is the first thing viewers will see. Open with the most characteristic thing in the subject\'s world, in the form that is most appropriate: a headline, an image, an animation, a live demo, an interactive moment, or other treatments. Be deliberate with your choice: a big number with a small label, supporting stats, and a gradient accent is the default treatment, so only use it if that\'s truly the best option.\\r\\n+Then implement working code (HTML/CSS/JS, React, Vue, etc.) that is:\\r\\n+- Production-grade and functional\\r\\n+- Visually striking and memorable\\r\\n+- Cohesive with a clear aesthetic point-of-view\\r\\n+- Meticulously refined in every detail\\r\\n \\r\\n-Typography carries the personality of the page. You don\'t need a different typeface for display or headline text and body content: use one family or two, and if two, make them clearly distinct.\\r\\n+## Frontend Aesthetics Guidelines\\r\\n \\r\\n-Choose your typefaces deliberately, not the default families you would reach for on any other project, and set a clear type scale following the default guidance of The Elements of Typographic Style with intentional weights, widths, and spacing. When type is used as a headline or visual element, use the type treatment itself as an active part of the design, not a neutral delivery vehicle for the content.\\r\\n+Focus on:\\r\\n+- **Typography**: Choose fonts that are beautiful, unique, and interesting. Avoid generic fonts like Arial and Inter; opt instead for distinctive choices that elevate the frontend\'s aesthetics; unexpected, characterful font choices. Pair a distinctive display font with a refined body font.\\r\\n+- **Color & Theme**: Commit to a cohesive aesthetic. Use CSS variables for consistency. Dominant colors with sharp accents outperform timid, evenly-distributed palettes.\\r\\n+- **Motion**: Use animations for effects and micro-interactions. Prioritize CSS-only solutions for HTML. Use Motion library for React when available. Focus on high-impact moments: one well-orchestrated page load with staggered reveals (animation-delay) creates more delight than scattered micro-interactions. Use scroll-triggering and hover states that surprise.\\r\\n+- **Spatial Composition**: Unexpected layouts. Asymmetry. Overlap. Diagonal flow. Grid-breaking elements. Generous negative space OR controlled density.\\r\\n+- **Backgrounds & Visual Details**: Create atmosphere and depth rather than defaulting to solid colors. Add contextual effects and textures that match the overall aesthetic. Apply creative forms like gradient meshes, noise textures, geometric patterns, layered transparencies, dramatic shadows, decorative borders, custom cursors, and grain overlays.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Default to line lengths of less than 80 characters. Serif typefaces can have slightly longer line lengths; give serif body text slightly more line-height than a sans-serif.\\r\\n+NEVER use generic AI-generated aesthetics like overused font families (Inter, Roboto, Arial, system fonts), cliched color schemes (particularly purple gradients on white backgrounds), predictable layouts and component patterns, and cookie-cutter design that lacks context-specific character.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Avoid these default typographic treatments; they are the commonest tells of a generated page:\\r\\n-- Accenting just a single word or phrase in a headline, like putting one word in italic/bold or a different color.\\r\\n-- Using all caps for labels.\\r\\n-- Adding unnecessary typographic labels above content.\\r\\n+Interpret creatively and make unexpected choices that feel genuinely designed for the context. No design should be the same. Vary between light and dark themes, different fonts, different aesthetics. NEVER converge on common choices (Space Grotesk, for example) across generations.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Visual structure is information. Structural devices like outlines, borders, numbering, eyebrows, dividers, labels, etc., encode useful information about the content rather than decorate it. Many generic designs use numbered markers (01 / 02 / 03), but that\'s only appropriate if the content actually is a sequence — like a stepped process or a timeline. Before adding numbered markers, check the content really is a sequence.\\r\\n+**IMPORTANT**: Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision. Maximalist designs need elaborate code with extensive animations and effects. Minimalist or refined designs need restraint, precision, and careful attention to spacing, typography, and subtle details. Elegance comes from executing the vision well.\\r\\n \\r\\n-Use non-user-triggered motion sparingly and deliberately, only to draw attention. A single orchestrated moment — one page-load sequence or one reveal — lands better than scattered effects; fade-and-slide-up entrances on each section and hover transitions on every card are the generic default and read as AI-generated. Motion that answers a person\'s action (opening, expanding, confirming) is welcome when it shows what changed.\\r\\n-\\r\\n-Consider written content carefully. Often a design brief may not contain real content, and it\'s up to you to come up with copy and placeholder content. Copy can make a design feel as templated as the design itself. See the below section on writing for more guidance.\\r\\n-\\r\\n-## Process: plan, review against the brief, build, critique\\r\\n-\\r\\n-For calibration, AI-generated design right now clusters around some traits:\\r\\n-1. a warm cream background (near #F4F1EA) with a high-contrast serif display and a terracotta or warm-clay accent (often near #D97757 — Anthropic\'s own Claude-interaction accent, so on a user\'s brief it reads as a tell);\\r\\n-2. a near-black background with a single bright acid-green or vermilion accent;\\r\\n-3. a broadsheet-style layout with hairline rules, zero border-radius, and dense newspaper-like columns;\\r\\n-4. the SaaS-card kit: content chopped into identical rounded cards, one border-radius on everything regardless of hierarchy, the same soft grey shadow (rgba(0,0,0,.1)) under each, and gradient washes as decoration;\\r\\n-5. template chrome that appears whatever the subject: a tracked-out ALL-CAPS eyebrow label above every heading; meta strings joined with middle dots (\'A · B · C\'); labels built as \'WORD — fragment\' with a spaced em dash; tinted near-black (#0B0B0B, #111) standing in for black; a monospace face for small data labels; a \'→\' appended to link and button text.\\r\\n-\\r\\n-All traits are legitimate for some briefs, but they are defaults rather than choices, and they appear regardless of subject. Where the brief pins down a visual direction, follow it exactly — the brief\'s own words always win, including when it asks for one of these looks. Where it leaves an axis free, don\'t spend that freedom on one of these defaults. As with a hired human designer, there\'s often a careful balance between doing what you\'re good at and taking each project as a chance to experiment and learn.\\r\\n-\\r\\n-Work in two passes. First, brainstorm a short design plan based on the client\'s design brief: create a compact token system with color, type, layout, and principles.\\r\\n-- Color: describe the core base palette as 4–6 named hex values.\\r\\n-- Type: the typefaces and their roles.\\r\\n-- Layout: a layout concept…1314 tokens truncated…n.
